With most imposing ceremony they presented her with a beautiful, decorated silk mantle, bearing all their names, and placed a large title, or sign, in gilt letters over the front door of her home: " Aged Character-Nourishing Mother," or freely translated, " The Aged Mother Who Has Nurtured Noble Men." It was the proudest day of her life when these young men presented her with this token of their loving reverence and esteem. When she was laid to rest, not long after, the beautiful mantle was, at the earnest request of the Chinese, thrown as a pall over her coffin, and the scrolls, banners, and silken canopy were carried at the head of the procession, first to the church, and then to the cemetery.

毕业生与在校学生们对狄考文夫人在学院中所做贡献所怀的情感，在她六十岁生日、也是她最后一场重病之前，得到了恰如其分的表达。他们以极为隆重的仪式，向她赠送了一件绣有众人姓名、装饰精美的丝质披风，并在她家大门之上，悬挂了一块烫金大字的匾额："德配年高之母"，或可意译为"教养群英的高龄慈母"。当这些青年将这份饱含敬爱之情的礼物献给她时，那是她一生中最为骄傲的一天。不久之后，她安息主怀，遵照中国人的恳切心愿，那件美丽的披风被覆盖在她的棺木之上，作为殓布；那些锦幛、旗幡与丝质华盖，也被抬在送葬队伍的最前列，先送往教堂，而后送至墓园。

第六章 · Chapter 6 重重难处 Difficulties and Discouragements

THE foregoing chapter upon Mrs. Mateer's school work leaves much deserving of mention. No one can adequately portray the difficulties under which a Christian college was built up in China. Below are given some that confronted Mr. and Mrs. Mateer. Among these the most serious was Mrs. Mateer's lack from the first of vigorous health. This was not owing to climate, for Northern China has one of the best in the world; it was partly organic, partly due to her experiences on the voyage, but chiefly to the constant and tremendous strain of overwork.

前一章讲述狄考文夫人的办学工作，还有许多值得一提之处未曾道尽。没有人能充分描绘出在中国创办一所基督教学院所面临的重重困难。以下列举几项狄考文夫妇曾经面对的难处。其中最严重的，是狄考文夫人自始便体质欠佳。这并非气候之故，因为华北的气候堪称世界上最宜人的地区之一；这一部分是体质使然，一部分源于航海途中的经历，但最主要的原因，还是长期过度操劳所带来的巨大压力。

A fellow missionary says: " She offered herself to the service of her friends with a most generous devotion. I think it is not too much to say that she gave herself a sacrifice for the people to whom she devoted her life. Her sympathy with all who came to her bringing their trials and burdens of sorrow, and her constant thought how she could help and spare others was a burden she gladly bore, but I fear it often made a heavy call upon exhausted nerves and strength."

一位宣教同工说："她以极为慷慨的献身精神，将自己奉献给朋友们的需要。我想毫不夸张地说，她是为她所奉献一生的这些人牺牲了自己。凡是带着难处和悲伤重担来找她的人，她都以同情相待；她始终想着如何能帮助别人、为别人分忧——这本是她甘心承受的重担，但恐怕也常常向她那疲惫的心力和体力提出沉重的要求。"

Another says, concerning a visit to the home of Mrs. Mateer: " I learned to sympathize with the disciples who went aside to rest. They were hoping for an uninterrupted communion with Jesus, only to find that the multitudes had outrun them, and were waiting on the other side. Our visit was constantly interrupted by schoolboys, teachers, evangelists, Chinese women and children, as well as missionaries, seeking the advice and help never denied them; yet her brave, overwhelmingly busy and cheerful life helped me more than any hour of conversation could have done."

另一位提到一次拜访狄考文夫人家的经历，说："我这才体会到那些退到一旁休息的门徒的心情。他们本盼望能与耶稣有一段不受打扰的团契时光，谁知众人竟抢先赶到，早已在对岸等候。我们的拜访也一再被打断——学生、教师、布道员、中国妇女和孩童，以及其他宣教士，纷纷前来寻求她的建议和帮助，而她从未拒绝过任何人；然而，她那英勇、忙碌到不堪重负却又始终愉悦的生活，给我的帮助，胜过任何一小时的交谈。"

Still another: " I remember Mrs. Mateer, who all her life in China suffered from ill health and so worked always at a disadvantage, remarking once that she felt like one of the mules in a mule-litter, with raw spots on his back, that had all day to plod along with his fearful load constantly rubbing his sores."

还有一位说："我记得狄考文夫人一生在中国都饱受病痛之苦，因此工作起来总是处于不利的境地。她曾说，自己就像骡轿里的一头骡子，背上磨出了生疮的伤口，却还要整日驮着可怕的重担蹒跚前行，伤口不断被摩擦。"

Once she confesses: " Certainly it would be far easier to be patient with others if one had no aching body or quivering nerves to worry her. Yesterday was cloudy and damp. Were I a heathen I should say it was dedicated to the God of Rheumatism. I heard my classes, and put in the rest of the day groaning." Again: " This year has been a trying one in the school. The chief teacher has done very badly, the younger teacher left after my return from the country and we have not been able to supply his place. Somehow, I have to spend the day in bed oftener and take more rest than I did last winter. I get clear tired of the Chinese and want to go home, but I have never kept that notion for more than one day." The wear of heathen surroundings and the continuous presence of the Chinese pressed heavily upon her. During a few days spent at Dr. Martin's mountain-retreat near Peking she writes: " How delightful it is, this pure mountain air, after the dust, filth and smells of the city, — real grass and great acres of it clothing the hillsides to their summits, oaks and maples such as I have not seen for years. I have never before since I came to China been for one whole day out of the sight and hearing of the Chinese. You can imagine how I enjoy this retreat where I can spend a half day at a time entirely alone if I choose."

有一次她坦承："若是没有病痛缠身、神经颤抖的困扰，对别人多点耐心确实会容易得多。昨天又阴又潮，若我是异教徒，一定会说这天专属于风湿之神。我照常上了课，剩下的时间都在呻吟中度过。"她又说："这一年学校里很不顺心。主教习表现很差，年轻的那位教习在我从乡下回来后就离开了，我们一直没能找到人接替他的位置。不知怎的，我比去年冬天更常需要卧床、需要更多休息。我对中国人感到彻底厌倦，只想回家——不过这种念头，我从没坚持超过一天。"异教环境的磨损，以及中国人无休无止的存在，都沉重地压在她身上。在北京附近丁韪良博士的山中别墅小住数日期间，她写道："这纯净的山间空气，比起城里的尘土、污秽和恶臭，是何等的怡人啊——真正的青草，成片覆盖着山坡直至山顶，还有多年未见的橡树和枫树。自我来华以来，我还从未有过一整天完全不见、不闻中国人的经历。你可以想见，我是多么享受这个可以随心所欲、独自静处半天的休养之所。"

The following gives a picture of one day's work: " As I cannot sleep for a while, I will write you. Before I came down-stairs this morning, the house woman came to say that her baby had been sick all night with croup, and asked me to get her some medicine. I quickly gave her some oil and camphor, together with some ipecac, and hurried her off home. A man was waiting to say that the chair-bearers of Mrs. Mateer, going to Weihsien, had struck for higher pay, and the servant who was to have gone with her flew up and had a mind not to go. A lot of time was spent in dickering, chair-bearers were secured and a servant hunted up. After breakfast we had Chinese and English letters to send off to Chefoo; then I went to see the sick baby. I had a hard history lesson to get and two classes to hear and three boys wanted eye medicine, one worm medicine, and six cough medicine, while a country brother wanted three kinds of medicines for his friends, and the chair-bearers all had to have some before they started, and it rained a little, and you may add to all this eight or ten Chinese standing looking on. Finally, at one o'clock Mrs. Mateer got off, and I rushed over to the girls' school to see Mrs. Shaw and get directions as to what needed attention during her visit in the country. There is a nasty scrape which needs to be settled. We decided that after Mrs. Shaw leaves I am to expel one girl, whip another, and give a good scolding and perhaps whipping to a third. I came home to find my arithmetic class waiting. After delivering messages to a number of pupils, I felt tired, and went up-stairs to see the baby and calm my nerves before supper. My days are not all like this, but simply very busy and very miscellaneous."

以下这段文字，描绘了她一天的工作情形："因为一时睡不着，我就给你写信。今早我还没下楼，帮佣的妇人就来说她的婴儿整夜因哮吼病发作，求我给点药。我赶紧给了她一些油和樟脑，还有一点吐根，打发她匆匆回家。接着有个男人在等着，说狄考文夫人去潍县的轿夫罢工要求加薪，本该随行的仆人也一时冒火，不想去了。费了好一番功夫讨价还价，才请到了轿夫，又找齐了仆人。早餐后，还有中英文信件要送去烟台；然后我去看那生病的婴儿。我还得备一堂难讲的历史课，听两班学生背诵，又有三个男孩要眼药、一个要打虫药、六个要止咳药，一位从乡下来的弟兄还要为朋友们讨三种药，轿夫们出发前也都要讨点药，天又下起小雨，此外还有八九个中国人站在一旁围观。终于，一点钟时，狄考文夫人出发了，我便匆匆赶去女校，找肖夫人商量她下乡探访期间需要留意的事项。有件棘手的纠纷需要处理，我们商定，肖夫人离开后，我要开除一个女孩，鞭打另一个，再狠狠训斥、也许再鞭打第三个。我回到家，算术班的学生已在等候。给几位学生传完话后，我觉得疲惫不堪，便上楼去看看婴儿，晚饭前先让神经平静下来。我的日子并非天天如此，但的确总是忙碌而杂乱。"

Mrs. Mateer's breakdowns were due to emergencies that laid on her additional burdens. The following is one illustration of many such experiences: During the summer of 1867 a horde of robbers from Honan, a remnant of the Tai Ping Rebellion, swept through Shantung Province. They produced such wide-spread panic that the country people fled for protection to the high-walled cities, or the mountains. These robbers killed, plundered and burned wherever they went. Tengchow City was alive with those who had taken refuge within its walls. They lodged in every conceivable place. The mission premises were crowded and Kwan Yin Tang was no exception. About seventy people lodged in the court, many of whom were sick. Mrs. Mateer gave her whole time to them. She doctored and nursed the sick, fed the hungry, comforted and preached to all. The great crowds and the exposure caused an epidemic of fever. Among those in the compound there were several cases to whom she gave such careful attention that not one died. Furthermore, her sister was ill during this time. The strain of all of this in addition to her regular work precipitated a breakdown from which she recovered only after a trip to Peking, and later to Shanghai for rest and care. One of the trying phases of this was the loss of her voice so that she could not speak above a whisper, and from this she suffered at various other times.

狄考文夫人的几次身体垮掉，都是因为突发状况给她加添了额外的重担。以下便是众多此类经历中的一个例子：1867年夏天，一伙来自河南的匪徒——太平天国之乱的余党——席卷了山东全省。他们造成了极大的恐慌，乡下百姓纷纷逃往高墙环绕的城池或山中避难。这些匪徒所到之处，烧杀抢掠，无所不为。登州城内挤满了前来避难的人，他们几乎在每一个可以栖身的角落安顿下来。差会的驻地也人满为患，观音堂也不例外。约有七十人挤在院子里，其中许多人生了病。狄考文夫人把全部时间都给了他们，为病人诊治护理，供饥饿者以食物，安慰众人、向众人传道。人群密集加上暴露的环境，引发了一场热病的流行。院内几个病例，因她悉心照料，竟无一人丧命。更何况，她的姐姐这段时间也病倒了。所有这一切加上她的日常工作，压力交加，终于使她的身体垮了下来，直到前往北京、后又赴上海休养调理，才得以恢复。这次病倒中最难熬的一段，是她的嗓子失了声，只能以气音低语，此后她也曾多次为此所苦。

Of the missionaries who came one after another to assist in the school, ten died, broke down in some way or other, or withdrew to be married. Each disappointment left the Mateers sorrowful and disconsolate, but they still held on, bent upon trying again. " Pressed on every side, yet not straitened; perplexed, yet not unto despair; pursued, yet not forsaken; smitten down, yet not destroyed."

先后前来协助学校的宣教士中，有十人或病故，或以某种方式垮掉，或因结婚而退出。每一次的失望，都让狄考文夫妇感到悲伤和沮丧，但他们仍然坚持下来，决心再度尝试。"四面受敌，却不被困住；心里作难，却不至失望；遭逼迫，却不被丢弃；打倒了，却不至死亡。"

For many long years the absence of physicians in Tengchow was an embarrassment, and an added burden. In the earlier years of mission work there were but few doctors who responded to the missionary call. Mrs. Mateer's own health suffered from lack of medical care, and from force of necessity she shouldered the burdensome responsibility and work of doctoring the boys, and many outside the school as well.

多年来，登州一直缺乏医生，这既令人尴尬，又是一种额外的负担。在宣教事工的早期，愿意响应呼召的医生寥寥无几。狄考文夫人自己的健康，就因为缺乏医疗照顾而受损，而出于不得已，她也只能一肩挑起为男孩们、乃至学校之外许多人诊病治病的繁重责任。

The school suffered on account of insufficient as well as old and inferior buildings, some of which must have been unsanitary. During the first years the main school building was used for church purposes, an arrangement that meant inconvenience and confusion. After about thirty years five thousand dollars was put into new school buildings, but this was only a short time before the college was moved to Weihsien. The Mateers were somewhat reconciled to such poor accommodations by the desire to keep their students in touch with the common people among whom it was hoped many of them would labor. They certainly had exceptional success in this direction, though the plant would have made a poor showing to a millionaire visitor from America. The outcome of the school indicates that in China it is better to err on the side of plainness and simplicity rather than to transplant the elaborate equipment, the pretension, and display of the West.

学校因为校舍不足、且陈旧简陋而深受其苦，其中有些校舍恐怕并不合乎卫生条件。最初几年，主要的校舍还兼作教堂之用，这样的安排带来了诸多不便和混乱。约三十年后，学校投入五千元建造新校舍，但没过多久，学院便迁往了潍县。狄考文夫妇之所以对如此简陋的条件多少能够释怀，是因为他们希望学生能够与普通百姓保持密切联系——他们盼望这些学生中许多人将来正是要在这样的百姓中间劳作。在这方面，他们确实取得了非凡的成功，尽管若有位来自美国的百万富翁来访，这样的校舍恐怕难登大雅之堂。这所学校的成果表明：在中国，宁可失之朴素简单，也不要移植西方那种精致的设备、排场和铺张。

The lack of books was serious but this did not daunt these missionaries. They were always champions of the idea that the whole college curriculum could and should be taught in the Chinese language. Drs. Mateer and Hayes prepared various scientific and mathematical books which are in extensive use, and taught others from manuscript. Mrs. Mateer assisted her sister, Mrs. Capp, in publishing a mental arithmetic; but her chief contribution in this field was the preparation of a hymn-and-tune book. She felt the importance of song among the Chinese, who as heathen never sing, but who are fond of music and can be taught to sing well. This book was found necessary and was wrought out in connection with her years of music teaching in the school, and also in the church, where she led the music until she had trained the choir. It is a statement of the principles of vocal music, in the form of questions and answers, followed by a series of progressive exercises, rounds, lays, etc., together with a large collection of church tunes and hymns. It uses a modified system of shaped notes which makes the acquisition of vocal music easier, while not impairing its use for the instrument. As this book had a wide circulation Mrs. Mateer later revised and stereotyped it.

书籍的匮乏是个严重的问题，但这并未使这些宣教士气馁。他们始终坚信，整个大学的课程都能够、也应当用中文来教授。狄考文博士和赫士博士编写了多种科学和数学书籍，如今被广泛使用，此外还用手稿讲授其他课程。狄考文夫人协助她的姐姐卡普夫人出版了一本心算教材；但她在这方面最主要的贡献，是编写了一本圣诗曲谱集。她深知歌唱对中国人的重要性——作为异教徒，他们向来不唱歌，但其实很喜爱音乐，也能够被教得唱得很好。这本书是她多年在学校教授音乐、又在教会中带领诗班（直到训练出合格的诗班为止）的过程中，逐渐感到必要而编写出来的。书中以问答的形式，阐述了声乐的基本原理，并附有一系列由浅入深的练习曲、轮唱曲、短歌等，另外还收录了大量教会曲调与圣诗。它采用了一套改良的图形音符体系，使学唱声乐变得更加容易，同时也不妨碍其用于乐器演奏。由于这本书流传甚广，狄考文夫人后来还对其进行了修订并制成了铅版。

The Chinese teachers at first were far from satisfactory. Western education was despised and foreigners hated, so it was almost impossible to secure good helpers. The best were ignorant of all approved pedagogy. For example, they expected the students to do all the work, they ruled entirely by fear, and in the presence of the boys praised the brighter and depreciated the duller ones. Custom called for older men who had spent their lives poring exclusively over the Confucian books and had come to revere Confucius as a god. To give up all of that and acknowledge that Confucius was only a poor sinner like themselves, in need of a Saviour, was shocking. Of course while more eloquent for Confucius than for Christ their religious influence could not be good. It is mentioned that one teacher who was quite satisfactory in the school was later found to be an opium-smoker and gambler, and was compelled to leave the city to escape bad debts. After great difficulty another was secured. This man's bodily presence was weak and contemptible but he came highly recommended. The boys received him as the frogs did their king, and no wonder! He was about as efficient as a log on a throne. But finally by dint of scolding and threatening he did wake up and get to work with a will.

中国教习起初远不能令人满意。西式教育被人轻视，外国人遭人憎恶，因此几乎不可能寻得优秀的助手。就算是最好的教习，也对一切公认的教学法一无所知。举例来说，他们指望学生包办一切事务，完全靠威吓来管教，并且当着学生的面，褒扬聪慧的、贬低迟钝的。按照惯例，他们所延请的多是把毕生精力都用来钻研儒家典籍、并将孔子奉若神明的年长之人。要他们放弃这一切，承认孔子不过是和他们一样的可怜罪人，也需要一位救主，实在令人震惊。当然，既然他们对孔子的推崇远胜于对基督，他们所能施加的宗教影响也就好不到哪里去。据载，曾有一位在校表现相当令人满意的教习，后来竟被发现是个抽鸦片、好赌博之徒，最终为了躲债不得不逃离本城。费了好大周折，才又请到另一位。这人体格瘦弱，仪表寒酸，却是有人极力推荐而来的。孩子们接纳他的态度，就像青蛙们接纳它们那位（寓言里的）国王一样——这也难怪！他的能耐大约就跟一段架在王座上的木头差不多。但最终，靠着不断的责骂和恫吓，他总算被惊醒过来，肯拿出干劲来做事了。

In view of all this, it can readily be seen how very important was Mrs. Mateer's close touch with the boys in counteracting such conditions. This state of things is now largely changed, and was so even in later years at Tengchow. Cultured Christian teachers of strong character and influence have been trained from childhood. The introduction of Western learning has been decreed by the emperor and is rapidly becoming popular, while in most cases correct religious ideas more fully control school sentiment.

有鉴于此，不难看出狄考文夫人与男孩们保持密切接触，对于抵消这类不良状况是何等重要。如今这种状况已大为改观，事实上，即便在登州办学的后期也已如此。品格高尚、极具影响力的基督徒教师，如今是从孩童时代起便受到栽培而成的。西学的引进，如今已由皇帝下诏推行，正迅速普及开来，而在大多数情况下，正确的宗教观念也更充分地左右着校园风气。

The boys, especially in the first years, were heathen and poverty stricken. Even those in China cannot appreciate the adverse conditions under which a school was conducted in the earlier years. The Chinese Church was in its infancy and not far removed from heathenism. The only people willing to brave the odium of sending their sons to a foreigner's school were the very poor, who prized education but could not supply the necessary food, clothing, books and tuition. The desperate poverty of many of the Chinese cannot be adequately described. The furnishing of one of their cheerless homes consists of about the following: a mud brick bed, having on it a little roll of bedding, one table and one little cupboard, a number of large jars and baskets of grain, a mill, two spinning-wheels and a basket of dishes, together with a donkey which occupies a corner of the outer room. Coming from such homes as these, boys were likely to be wretched specimens both physically and morally. They had probably been half starved and but half clothed, so that they were unfitted for success in study, and some sooner or later had, on account of this, to be dismissed. Indeed a certain number of the brightest and choicest boys on account of insufficient food in early years failed in health and died before completing their studies.

尤其是在办学最初那些年，男孩子们大多来自异教家庭，境况贫寒。即便是身在中国的人，也难以体会早年办学所面临的种种不利条件。当时的中国教会尚在襁褓之中，离异教信仰其实相距不远。唯有那些极为贫穷的人家，才愿意冒着世俗非议，把儿子送到外国人开的学校来——他们虽然珍视教育，却供应不起必需的食物、衣着、书籍和学费。许多中国人的赤贫景况，实在难以尽述。他们那些黯淡无光的家，家当大致不外乎：一张土坯砌成的炕，上面铺着一小卷铺盖，一张桌子，一个小柜橱，几只盛粮的大缸和篮筐，一台磨，两架纺车，一篮碗碟，此外还有一头驴，占据着外屋的一角。来自这样家庭的男孩，身心两方面往往都是些可怜的样本。他们很可能是半饥半饱、衣不蔽体地长大的，因此难以在学业上取得成功，有些人迟早也因此不得不被劝退。事实上，确有若干最聪慧、最出色的男孩，正因为幼年营养不良，健康每况愈下，未及完成学业便离世了。

It is not surprising that skin disease gave trouble in the school especially after vacation. Mrs. Mateer says: " I have found out that three boys have the itch. It was once in the school for three years. I doctored and worried and worked with the boys during all that time. While one was getting well two others were contracting it. Finally I threatened to whip the next boy who was found to have newly caught it, and to whip all who had it if not well within a certain time. This proved a specific and worked like a charm, especially when accompanied by a sulphur compound which was so vile-smelling that no one wished to come near the unfortunate who was anointed with it."

校内因皮肤病而生出的麻烦，尤其在假期过后，也就不足为奇了。狄考文夫人说："我发现有三个男孩长了疥疮。这病曾一度在学校里流行了整整三年。那段时间里，我不断给孩子们治病、操心、忙碌。往往是一个刚好些，另外两个又染上了。最后我放出话来，谁被发现新染上此病，就要挨鞭子，若限期内不痊愈，凡患病者一律挨打。这一招果然灵验，效果奇佳——尤其是再配上一种硫磺合剂，那气味实在难闻，谁都不愿靠近那个被涂抹了此药的倒霉孩子。"

Moral delinquencies found expression in various ways. One boy would accuse the cook of graft in hope of getting the position for a friend; another would be convicted of stealing; another would be indolent and worthless; while still another would become so prominent as a young baptized enthusiast as to claim special liberty in violation of the rules, and being punished for this would sulk, and having lost the glory for religious zeal would leave school.

品行上的过失，也以各种方式表现出来。有个男孩为了让朋友能顶替某个职位，便诬告厨子贪污；另一个则被查出偷窃；还有一个游手好闲、一无是处；而另有一人，则因作为一名年轻的受洗信徒而颇为出风头，竟以为自己可以特权违反校规，受罚之后便闹脾气，因失去了那份宗教热忱所带来的光彩而干脆离校了事。

Some would contract debts. At one time this became a mania, nearly every boy after speculating in borrowing and lending having some outstanding debt. One of Mrs. Mateer's pupils, now an honored teacher, says that when in college he borrowed some money from her, and like many other Chinese who borrowed from the missionaries, had no idea of repaying it. After some months he called to see her. Just as he was leaving, she said to him: " I have been thinking about a passage in the Scripture. I wonder if you have ever thought of it. Please turn to Romans xiii. 8 (' Owe no man anything '), and tell me just what you think it really means." She did it so kindly, he said, that he could not refuse to read it. He read it over, felt the color coming to his cheek, resolved then and there to grind on the hand mill or do anything to earn money, pay the debt, and never allow himself to be again in such an embarrassing position. He carried out his resolve and now after thirty years rejoices in having no debts hanging over his head.

有些人则欠下债务。有一阵子，这竟成了一种风气，几乎每个男孩都热衷于借贷投机，人人身上都背着未还的债。狄考文夫人的一名学生——如今已是位受人尊敬的教师——说，他在大学期间曾向她借过一笔钱，和许多向宣教士借钱的中国人一样，压根没打算偿还。几个月后，他去拜访她。就在他要告辞的时候，她对他说："我一直在思想圣经里的一段经文，不知你可曾想过。请翻到罗马书13章8节（'凡事都不可亏欠人'），告诉我你觉得这话到底是什么意思。"他说，她说这话时是那样和蔼，令他无法拒绝去读。他读了一遍，只觉双颊发烫，当场便下定决心，哪怕去推磨，也要想方设法挣钱还债，绝不再让自己陷入这般难堪的境地。他果然说到做到，如今三十年过去，他很欣慰自己已再无债务缠身。

Another boy had a quarrel with his father, and attempted suicide. He went out on some pretext and bought a large dose of opium which he took as soon as he returned. A student saw him wiping a suspicious-looking powder off his lips, and heard him saying something about " his troubles being soon over," so he reported him. An emetic was mixed, but he refused to take it. It was not the time to coax and parley, so a brisk application of the ruler changed his mind. He was soon past danger, though ill for two or three days. Later, Dr. Mateer gave him a severe whipping in the presence of the school. This was an experiment, but it proved successful, as it brought him to his senses, and nothing more was heard of the wrong done him by saving his life.

另有一个男孩，因与父亲争吵，竟企图自尽。他借故外出，买了一大剂鸦片，一回来便服了下去。一名学生看见他嘴角擦拭着一种可疑的粉末，又听见他嘟囔着"他的烦恼很快就要结束了"之类的话，便报告了此事。众人调制了催吐剂，他却不肯服用。事已至此，容不得再好言相劝，于是猛地用戒尺一顿，他这才回心转意。他很快便脱离了危险，只是病了两三天。后来，狄考文博士当着全校的面，狠狠地鞭打了他一顿。这本是一次尝试，却收到了成效，使他幡然醒悟；而因救了他一命所加于他身上的这份"委屈"，此后也再无人提起。

For various reasons boys would run away from the school. Sometimes it was their own idea, sometimes that of their home people. A young missionary who spent his first month in the Mateer home says that he vividly recollects a scene in the front yard, viewed from his window. A schoolboy was being pulled towards the gate by his father, while Mrs. Mateer gripped the boy on the other side to retain him in the school. The struggle was long continued, but finally she got the boy back.

出于种种原因，也有男孩从学校逃跑。有时是他们自己的主意，有时是家里人的意思。一位在狄考文家中度过初到中国第一个月的年轻宣教士说，他仍清楚记得从自己窗口望见前院的一幕：一个学生正被父亲拉向大门，狄考文夫人则从另一边抓住这孩子，要把他留在学校。这场拉锯持续了很久，但最终她还是把孩子夺了回来。

Another boy became frightened by reason of some wild rumor and ran away. It was said the missionaries had medicine that would bewitch, that some day they would put the boys on board a ship and take them to America, where they would make silver out of their eyes, opium out of their hearts, and other kinds of medicines out of other parts of their bodies. Many believed such stories, nearly all thought there was some truth in them, and it was not strange this boy had become frightened. He was the son of a wretched opium-smoker, and when he came to school he had not had a suit of clothes for two years, nor any protection from the cold of winter except an old ragged rug, nor had he been beyond the miserable court where he lived. An older brother and also a sister had been at the school telling him these rumors that frightened him, and making plans for his escape, which was soon effected. Mrs. Mateer says: " After an early dinner I went in my chair to pay the married sister a visit, having been assured that she knew where the boy was. I told her I had come for her brother, and that she must find him. Over and over she averred that she was not his sister, but only his cousin. I thought that even a Chinese woman could not lie so, and concluded I must be in the wrong place, so I sent for the school steward who assured me that this was the home of the sister.

另一个男孩，因听信某种荒诞的谣言而受了惊吓，逃跑了。当时传说宣教士有能施妖术的药，说有朝一日会把男孩们送上船带去美国，在那里从他们的眼睛里炼出银子，从他们的心里炼出鸦片，从他们身体的其他部位炼出别的药物。许多人相信这类传言，几乎人人都以为其中或有几分真实，因此这男孩心生恐惧，也就不足为怪了。他是一个可怜的鸦片鬼的儿子，来上学时已经两年没有一套像样的衣服，除了一床破烂的旧毯子，再没有别的东西可以御寒，也从未走出过他所住的那个凄惨的院落。他的一位兄长和一位姐姐都曾到学校来，向他讲述这些吓人的传言，还替他谋划逃跑，不久便得逞了。狄考文夫人说："吃过早饭后，我便乘轿前去拜访那位出嫁的姐姐，因为有人向我保证她知道那男孩的下落。我告诉她，我是来找她弟弟的，她必须把他找出来。她一再声称自己不是他姐姐，只是他表姐。我心想，就算是中国妇女，也不至于撒这样的谎，便以为自己是不是找错了地方，于是派人去请学校的管事来，他却肯定地告诉我，这确实就是姐姐的家。

" When I became tired, I took out my book and read to show that I was in earnest and meant to wait for the boy, but this did not make much impression. The neighbors came in to assure me that she was only the boy's cousin, and that he had not been to see her for months. We talked 'doctrine,' and we talked about many other things, but she showed no signs of yielding. Whenever the conversation flagged I took up my book. After a long time she sent for the older brother. He could lie as glibly as she, but not so shrewdly. I sent for my supper, which I shared with my hostess and her son. Then I lighted my candle and returned to my book. The brother lay down on a pile of fuel and was soon snoring. The woman got sick, grew rapidly worse, and told her son to run for some of her friends, for she was sick to the point of death. She began to scream, but her brother snored on. I stopped reading long enough to suggest a dose of soda to relieve her stomach. I did not get frightened and she did not die, but was soon quite well.

我等得疲倦时，便拿出书来读，好表明我是认真的，打定主意要等到那男孩出现——但这似乎并没起多大作用。邻居们纷纷进来向我担保，说她只是那男孩的表姐，而且他已经好几个月没来看过她了。我们谈了'教义'，也谈了许多别的事情，但她始终不见有让步的迹象。每当谈话冷场，我便拿起书来读。过了好久，她派人去叫来了那位兄长。他撒起谎来和她一样油滑，却不如她那般精明。我叫人送来晚饭，与女主人和她儿子一同分食。随后，我点起蜡烛，重新拿起书来读。那兄长躺在一堆柴火上，不一会儿便打起鼾来。那妇人忽然"病"了，而且病势急转直下，让她儿子快去请她的一些朋友来，说她病得快要死了。她开始尖叫起来，她兄长却依旧鼾声不断。我停下阅读，只是建议给她喝点苏打水缓解胃部不适。我并未惊慌，她也没有死，不久便完全康复了。

" Finally I concluded that I had enough of it, and sent the steward to suggest to Mr. Mateer that he come and take the brother prisoner. This he did, and about nine o'clock my visit ended. The woman was as polite to me all afternoon as if I had been the most distinguished and welcome guest, never for a moment betraying what I knew was the feeling of her heart. Even after my husband had dragged her brother off to prison, she did not forget her politeness, but accompanied me out to the chair, expressing regrets that I had been put to so much trouble. We kept the prisoner for four or five days, when he got tired and sent for his uncle, who soon found the runaway." Nothing but an unfailing sense of humor could have carried Mrs. Mateer through this and many similar experiences.

最后，我认定自己受够了，便打发管事去向狄考文先生示意，请他来把那兄长当场拿下。他果然这样做了，大约九点钟，我这场拜访便结束了。那妇人整个下午对我都是彬彬有礼，仿佛我是位极受尊敬、备受欢迎的贵客一般，丝毫没有流露出我心知肚明的真实心思。即便我丈夫已经把她兄长拖去关押，她仍不忘礼数，送我出门直到轿前，还表示歉意，说给我添了这么多麻烦。我们扣押那人质四五天后，他终于厌倦了，便派人去请他的叔叔来，很快便找到了那逃跑的男孩。"若非有一副不曾失灵的幽默感，狄考文夫人恐怕难以撑过这一次，以及许多类似的经历。

The kitchen for the boys repeatedly produced a crop of troubles. On the day the school first opened the cook who had been employed declined to undertake the job. The one secured, instead, grew tired of the boys, and after the first month could not be persuaded to remain. " In April there was a quarrel about the rice. The boys complained that the food was not so good as it should be for the allowance. The cook said he used all the money for the food. After investigation a regular bill of fare was made. The boys were scolded, and one whipped, when once more things settled down peacefully."

男孩们的伙房也一再引发一连串的麻烦。学校刚开办那天，原本雇来的厨子却打了退堂鼓，不肯就任。后来请到的那位，倒是渐渐厌倦了这些男孩，第一个月过后，便怎么也劝不住他留下来了。"四月里为了大米闹了一场纠纷。男孩们抱怨，按他们的份例，伙食本该更好才是。厨子则说，他把所有的钱都用在了伙食上。经过调查，制定出了一份正式的食谱。男孩们挨了训，还有一个挨了打，这才又重新恢复了平静。"

After a few years it is recorded that for more than a year there had been much dissatisfaction with the cook, especially on the part of the larger boys. He was suspicious and censorious, making no allowance for the faults and folly of youth. The boys were equally suspicious and took no account of his cares and weariness. The suggestion was made that if the boys had a little more employment and some experience of the care and labor required to provide their food, they would have more forbearance and less time to make trouble. Accordingly, the experiment was for a time adopted of having the boys buy their own provisions, saw the wood, carry the water, set the table, etc. They were delighted. The cook was not faultless, but the boys were too anxious to make the new plan succeed to find fault about small things. The cook would occasionally be accused of appropriating money, and the boy at the head of the family would now and then abstract some, but these difficulties grew less as time went on.

又过了几年，据记载，一年多以来，孩子们——尤其是年纪较大的那些——对厨子一直颇有怨言。他多疑而爱挑剔，全然不体谅少年人的过失和荒唐；男孩们同样也疑心重重，丝毫不顾及他的辛劳和疲惫。于是有人提议，若能让男孩们多做些事，亲身体验一下备办伙食所需的操劳和心力，他们或许就会多几分体谅，少几分惹是生非。于是，学校一度试行了这样的办法：由男孩们自己采买食材、劈柴、挑水、摆桌等等。他们对此欣喜不已。厨子固然不算完美，但男孩们一心盼着这个新办法能够成功，反倒不去计较那些鸡毛蒜皮的小事了。厨子偶尔仍会被指责挪用钱款，负责这一伙食的男孩头头，有时也难免中饱私囊，但随着时间推移，这类纠纷渐渐减少了。

Mrs. Mateer had frequent opportunity to exercise tact in getting over rough places. On one occasion her cook bought a three-stringed fiddle and kept sawing away monotonously and discordantly to her great annoyance. At last some particularly excruciating strains brought her out to ask him what such a fiddle cost. " Why do you ask?" said he. " Because I want to buy one." " I will go out at once on the street and purchase one for you." " No," said Mrs. Mateer, " I think I should like to buy this one." " Why desire this one specially?" " I want to buy it because I think it should have a rest."

狄考文夫人也常有机会，凭机智化解一些棘手的场面。有一回，她的厨子买了一把三弦琴，成天单调刺耳地拉个不停，令她大为苦恼。终于，几声格外刺耳的琴音，把她引了出来，问他这样一把琴要花多少钱。"你问这个做什么？"他说。"因为我也想买一把。""那我这就去街上给您买一把来。""不，"狄考文夫人说，"我想我倒愿意买你手上这一把。""怎么偏偏想要这一把呢？""我想买下它，是觉得它也该歇一歇了。"

National disturbances twice broke up the school for a time. In June, 1870, the Tientsin massacre of Catholics, and the burning of their property occurred. The country was filled with rumors of plottings in other directions. The murmurings increased and the Chinese in large numbers caught at the idea, always just below the surface in China, of driving out the hated foreigners. It was decided that the missionaries should go to Chefoo on a war vessel, so it was finally necessary to dismiss the school. Mrs. Mateer wrote to her sister: " Our poor boys — when will we get them all back again? It seems to me that one of the ends of this day's trial to us is to teach us to live more day by day. We were settled down there in a good climate, in a comfortable home, with at present very good health, plenty and more than plenty of delightful work, which by God's blessing had been prospering more and more year by year. Of course we know this is not our abiding place, but it was hard to realize it. I felt that I was loving the work for the work's sake, forgetful of the great end of our work and the Master who appointed it. What I knew not how to remember, God knew. We cannot see at once the whole of the room we are standing in. We do not get the proper impression of a painting when too near it. So perhaps we have our work taken from us for a while that we may see beyond the work to its great end, or rather we are driven away from the work to show that the work is permanent, we are the accidents."

国内的两场动荡，也曾一度使学校被迫停办。1870年6月，天津发生了屠杀天主教徒并焚毁其财产的惨案。全国各地流言四起，都说别处也在酝酿类似的阴谋。骚动愈演愈烈，大批中国人趁机附和那股在中国始终潜伏于表面之下的驱逐"可恨的洋人"的念头。于是决定，宣教士们应当乘坐一艘军舰前往烟台，因此学校最终不得不解散。狄考文夫人写信给她的姐姐说："我们那些可怜的孩子——我们什么时候才能把他们都接回来呢？在我看来，这一日的试炼，其用意之一，正是要教导我们更加脚踏实地、按日而活。我们本已在那里安顿下来，气候宜人，居所舒适，眼下身体也十分健康，工作既丰富又令人欣喜，蒙神赐福，年复一年地愈发兴旺。当然，我们知道这里并非我们永久的居所，但要真正体会到这一点，却并不容易。我发觉自己爱这工作，竟只是为了工作本身而爱，忘却了我们工作的伟大目的，也忘却了指派这工作的那位主。我不知该如何铭记于心的，神却知道。我们身处一间屋子之中，无法一眼看尽全貌；离一幅画太近，也无法获得正确的印象。因此，或许我们的工作被暂时收回，正是要让我们看清工作之外那更伟大的目的；又或者，我们被迫暂离工作，恰恰是要显明这工作本身是恒久的，而我们不过是偶然的过客罢了。"

During the Japanese war of 1895, Tengchow was bombarded as a ruse to divert the Chinese from Wei-hai-wei, the fortified harbor taken by the Japanese. This repeated bombardment in this sleepy old town of course caused great excitement. The boys had just gone home on their New Year's vacation, but were detained from returning for about one month after the time of term opening. The women in alarm rushed to the foreign compound in great numbers. Mrs. Mateer, together with a few others, did not go to Chefoo, refusing to leave her husband and the Christians. She comforted and quieted the crowds who came, herself rising into the serenity of an all-conquering faith. Her manner and her message while the cannon-balls were flying over and lighting near made a profound impression. Dr. and Mrs. Mateer thus helped care for the Christians and talk to the crowd during the day, and went to be with the girls in the school during the night, the girls not being able to get away to their homes for some time after the bombardment.

1895年中日战争期间，登州遭到炮击，那不过是日军为了转移中国人对威海卫——那处已被日军攻占的设防海港——注意力所使出的一计。这座沉睡的老城屡遭炮轰，自然引起极大的恐慌。孩子们刚回家过完新年假期，开学之期已到，却被耽搁了约一个月才得以返校。惊慌的妇女们纷纷涌入外国人的驻地。狄考文夫人和另外几人并未前往烟台，因为她不愿撇下丈夫和信徒们。她安慰、平息了前来避难的人群，自己则升华至一种战无不胜的信心所带来的从容之中。炮弹在头顶呼啸而过、在附近炸响之时，她的态度与话语，都给人留下了深刻的印象。狄考文夫妇就这样，白天照顾信徒、安抚民众，夜晚则前去陪伴女校的学生——轰炸之后，那些女生有很长一段时间都无法回到自己家中。

In the enduring and curing of all these various adverse conditions there is the shining out of Mrs. Mateer's splendid qualities of mind and heart. Her self-control, her grace, her long, steadfast devotion to duty, both under the difficult conditions of the daily routine and in the hour of imminent danger, mark her as one more example of the splendid fruitage of the Puritan home.

在忍受并克服这一切种种逆境的过程中，狄考文夫人心智与品格中那卓越的品质，也由此得以彰显。她的自制、她的优雅、她对本分那长久而坚定不移的忠诚——无论是在日常琐事的艰难处境之下，还是在危险迫近的时刻——都标志着她是清教徒家风所结出的又一硕果。

第七章 · Chapter 7 收获果实 Fruit Gathering

AS former chapters show, this school work was by no means all discouragement. Even from the first the boys were not all, nor always, so disappointing, while the religious character and moral tone constantly improved. As early as '67 Mrs. Mateer records: " At the close of the previous year the Christian boys were much impressed with the injury done to the school and the cause of Christ by the bad conduct of the Li boys, and the heresy of the old teacher. They entered into a concert of prayer for an outpouring of the Holy Spirit on their teachers, on their own hearts, and on the school at large. It was begun for a month but was continued during the whole year." Thus conditions improved throughout the history of the school, until after thirty years nearly all the one hundred undergraduates and all the sixty-seven graduates were professing Christians.

正如前几章所示，办学工作绝非只有令人沮丧之事。即便在最初，孩子们也并非个个、或始终都令人失望，而学校的信仰品格与道德风气也在不断提升。早在1867年，狄考文夫人便记述道："去年年底，基督徒学生们深深感受到，李家兄弟的不良行为以及那位老教习的异端之说，给学校和基督的事业带来了伤害。他们于是发起了一个联合祷告，为教师、为自己的心、也为整个学校，祈求圣灵浇灌。这原本只打算持续一个月，却延续了整整一年。"就这样，学校的境况在其历史进程中持续改善，三十年后，将近一百名在校生、以及全部六十七名毕业生，都成了公开宣信的基督徒。

The founders of the school always planned to furnish a thorough education in the Chinese language as well as in Western branches, so that graduates would be well fitted for various important positions, and hence none would enter the ministry unless really called of God. In this they were eminently successful. Many turned to other vocations, but as many became pastors as the people at that time were ready to call. The splendid consecration of our Shantung pastors is shown in the comparatively low salaries they are willing to accept from the Chinese Church. Some of them would be able to command from five to ten times as much in other vocations. Pastors in Western countries are seldom called upon to make such sacrifices. All this is eloquent in praise of those who projected and successfully wrought out an educational policy in Shantung.

学校的创办者始终计划，既要在国学、也要在西学方面提供扎实的教育，使毕业生足以胜任各种重要职位，因此，唯有真正蒙神呼召的人，才会投身牧职。在这一点上，他们取得了极大的成功。许多人转向了其他行业，但成为牧师的人数，也恰好与当时教会所能延请的数目相当。我们山东牧师们那令人钦佩的献身精神，正体现在他们甘愿接受中国教会所能负担的、相对微薄的薪金上。他们当中有些人，若从事其他行业，收入本可高出五倍到十倍。西方国家的牧师，极少被要求作出这样的牺牲。凡此种种，都是对那些在山东策划并成功推行这一教育方针之人的有力赞颂。

In 1890, five graduates were ordained to the ministry. For years they had been thoroughly tested as teachers and evangelists. It was fitting that Dr. Mateer should be moderator of this presbytery, and not strange that the tears ran down his face as he laid his hands upon their heads. Mrs. Mateer's face showed her joy in the consecration of these " sons." They had come as little heathen boys, and for a quarter of a century she had given them the best she knew.

1890年，五位毕业生被按立为牧师。多年来，他们作为教师和布道员，都经过了充分的考验。狄考文博士担任这次长老会按立仪式的主席，可谓恰如其分；当他把手按在他们头上时，泪水顺着他的脸颊流下，也就不足为奇了。狄考文夫人脸上，则流露出她对这些"儿子们"献身的喜悦。他们曾是异教家庭中的懵懂男童，二十五年来，她把自己所知的一切最好的东西，都给了他们。

There would have been six to be ordained had not one died a few days before his ordination, but his earnest, consecrated life had already borne much fruit, and his story is one of deep interest.

本该有六人受按立，只因其中一人在按立前几天病故；但他那真诚、献身的一生，早已结出丰硕的果实，他的故事，也是一段极为动人的经历。

" Hing Dao Wing was a member of the very first class, gathered from out a purely heathen world, at the opening of the Tengchow school in 1864. Neither these boys nor their parents had ever heard of the true God or of Jesus Christ, or of the Bible or of a church or of a heaven or hell. The parents would not sign an indenture covering more than five or six years. This was not strange, for the people did not know much about the missionaries, and the wild rumors already referred to were half believed by many.

"邢道荣是1864年登州学堂开办之初、第一批学生中的一员，那批学生完全是从异教世界中招募而来的。这些孩子和他们的父母，都从未听说过真神，也未听说过耶稣基督，未听说过圣经、教会，或天堂地狱。家长们不肯签订超过五六年期限的契约。这也不足为奇，因为百姓当时对宣教士所知甚少，前文提到的那些荒诞传言，也有许多人半信半疑。

" In consequence Hing Dao Wing was not allowed to return after his sixth year. However, he had become a professing Christian. The very first New Year visit home he hid, being willing to risk a severe beating for hiding rather than worship idols.

"因此，邢道荣满六年后便不获准再回校。然而，他此时已成为一名公开宣信的基督徒。头一次新年回家探亲时，他躲了起来，宁可冒着挨一顿毒打的风险躲避，也不愿去拜偶像。

" Soon after joining the church he promised to give one thousand cash, equal to one dollar, to help in sending an evangelist to preach in his village. He never had twenty-five cents a year pocket money. When asked, ' Where will you get one thousand cash to give?' he answered, ' I shall grind and earn it.' That meant that he would walk round and round a hand mill, pushing the millstone by a handle, till he had ground eighteen bushels of corn. This he did and paid the money promptly.

"入教不久后，他便许诺捐出一千文钱，相当于一块银元，用以资助派遣一名布道员到他的村子传道。他一年到头都不曾有过二毛五分钱的零用钱。有人问他：'你从哪里弄来这一千文钱？'他答道：'我去推磨挣来。'这意味着他要绕着一台石磨，握着磨杆一圈又一圈地推，直到磨完十八蒲式耳（约合六百多斤）的玉米。他果然这样做了，并如期如数付清了这笔钱。

" A few years after he was taken out of school, his aunt died. According to Chinese custom all of her sons and nephews must go to the temple to pray for her and also bow down at her coffin and worship her spirit. Not to do this would be a great offense to her, so his friends were determined to make Hing Dao Wing perform this worship. He had grown so cold by this time that he consented to bow down at the coffin, quieting his conscience by refusing to go to the temple. For this worship, and for working on the Sabbath, he was suspended from church-membership. We were sorely grieved about him, for he had seemed a sincere Christian, and we had fondly hoped that some day he would be a minister. We did not give him up but kept on praying that God would make him a good man, bring him back to school and call him to be a minister.

"他离校几年后，他的一位姑母去世了。按照中国的习俗，她的所有儿子和侄子，都必须到庙里为她祷告，还要在她灵柩前叩拜、祭拜她的亡灵。若不这样做，便是对亡者极大的不敬，因此他的亲友都执意要邢道荣行这祭拜之礼。此时他的信心已经冷淡到，竟同意在灵柩前叩拜，只是靠着拒绝进庙来安慰自己的良心。因为这次祭拜，也因为他在安息日做工，他被停止了教籍。我们为他深感忧伤，因为他一向显得是位真诚的基督徒，我们也曾满心盼望，有朝一日他能成为一名牧师。但我们并未放弃他，仍不断祷告，求神使他成为一个良善之人，将他带回学校，并呼召他成为牧师。

" Having tried in vain to obtain his people's consent to his returning to school, finally after six years he took the law into his own hands and came back to finish his studies and work for his Saviour. He studied diligently and became a fine scholar, teaching a year in the college. But he felt called to preach the Gospel as an evangelist, so although highly prizing him as a teacher, we could not object to his going.

"他曾多次尝试征得家人同意让他回校，都是徒劳，最终，在六年之后，他索性自作主张，回来完成学业，为他的救主效力。他勤奋治学，成了一名优秀的学者，并在学院任教一年。但他觉得自己蒙召要作布道的传道人，因此，尽管我们十分珍视他这位教师，也不能反对他离开。

" He preached at thirty Christian stations under Dr. Nevius for two years, then entered the theological class. During this time of study he spent five months of each year in evangelistic work in the Weihsien field. One fall, in company with one of the missionaries, he preached in two hundred villages, going to eight or ten daily. This was hard work. Often scholars came out and scowled at the crowd on the street so that all dispersed. The sentiment was so strong against the foreigner that the villages would not supply a place to rest at noon, nor warm water to make tea, so these two ate Chinese hardtack and a turnip out along the road in a place sheltered from the cold wind.

"他在倪维思博士座下，于三十处布道站传道两年，而后进入神学班学习。求学期间，他每年都要抽出五个月，在潍县教区从事布道工作。有一年秋天，他与一位宣教士同行，在两百个村庄传道，每天走访八到十个村子。这是极为辛苦的工作。常有读书人出来对街上聚集的人群怒目而视，众人便一哄而散。当地排外的情绪如此强烈，以至于村里既不肯提供一处歇脚之地，也不肯供应热水沏茶，于是这两人只能沿路找一处避风之地，就着几块中国干粮和一根萝卜充饥。

" Through all this Mr. Hing was unfailingly cheerful and always ready for all the discouragement and weariness connected with this campaign. He was an attractive and consecrated man and his death was sorely mourned by many."

"在这一切之中，邢先生始终不改乐观开朗之态，甘愿承受这场布道行程所带来的种种挫折与疲惫。他是一位极具魅力、全然献身之人，他的离世，令许多人深感哀痛。"

Our Shantung pastors are forceful, evangelical preachers. They are bright, enterprising and enthusiastic in their work and the churches under their care are rapidly growing in numbers and intelligence.

我们山东的牧师们，都是极具感召力的福音派讲道人。他们在事工上聪敏、进取、满怀热忱，他们所牧养的教会，无论在人数还是在信仰的领悟上，都在迅速增长。

Rev. Ting Li Mei is an example of the Tengchow students who have given their lives to the ministry. His family was of some standing. They had planned for their unusually gifted son prominence in government employ and were bitterly opposed to his entering the Christian ministry. However the Spirit of God prevailed and in due time he became the pastor of a congregation. The Boxer outbreak overtook him, but he stayed with his flock. As the leader, he was seized, beaten and left for dead, but like Paul he revived. Again they ordered him beaten because he would not deny his Saviour, and he was rescued by the missionaries through the influence of the United States Consul, Mr. Fowler, with the governor, Yuan Shi Kai.

丁立美牧师，便是登州学生中献身牧职的一个典范。他家境不俗，家人本打算让这位天资过人的儿子在仕途上出人头地，因此强烈反对他投身基督教的牧职。然而，神的灵终究得胜，他后来按时成了一处会众的牧师。义和团之乱爆发时，他也未能幸免，但仍与他的羊群同在。作为带领者，他被抓获、遭到毒打，被弃于一旁，以为已死，却如保罗一般苏醒过来。他们又下令再次殴打他，只因他不肯背弃自己的救主；后来，靠着美国领事福勒先生（Mr. Fowler）向巡抚袁世凯所施加的影响，宣教士们才将他救出。

He built up different congregations, but his strength of character, together with his great faith and ardent zeal, led him away from a single congregation to the conduct of revival meetings in many places in North China. Thousands have been quickened and thousands brought into the kingdom through his preaching and prayers. He knows nothing of the modern uncertainty and hesitation in his intensely earnest and interesting presentation of the great spiritual truths of sin and salvation, while his prayers move the very throne of God.

他曾建立过好几处会众，但他坚强的品格，加上他伟大的信心和炽热的热忱，使他离开了单一牧区，转而在华北各地主领奋兴布道会。经由他的讲道与祷告，成千上万的人心得以复苏，成千上万的人归入了神的国度。在阐述罪与救恩这些伟大的属灵真理时，他讲得极为恳切、生动，对现代人常有的那种迟疑不定一无所知，而他的祷告，更是能撼动神的宝座。

Mr. Ting writes the following: " I once asked Mrs. Mateer to loan me some money. ' For what purpose?' said she. ' To buy a small coat,' I replied. She asked when and how I purposed to repay her, which I could not answer. ' Better allow me to propose a plan. You come daily and care for my flowers. I will loan you the money and credit you with the work. You will thus be free from debt, and I shall have my flowers cared for.' So wise was all her helpfulness.

丁先生写道："有一次，我请狄考文夫人借我一些钱。'做什么用？'她问。'买一件小褂子，'我答道。她又问我打算何时、如何偿还，我却答不上来。'不如让我来出个主意。你每天来照料我的花草，我把这笔钱借给你，把你做的工算作抵偿。这样一来，你就不欠债了，我的花草也有人照料了。'她所给予的每一份帮助，都是这般充满智慧。

" One day she gave me nine hundred cash, asking me to take it to a friend of hers who lived in the ' Water-City,' north of the city proper, in the ninth house from the south end, on the street facing the west bank of the river. Following these minute directions, I found Mrs. Mateer's friend, who proved to be extremely poor. Moreover, he was partially paralyzed; his eyes, mouth, hands and feet were twisted, and he walked with great difficulty as he went about begging for food. This unfortunate Mrs. Mateer considered her friend, and sent me specially to visit him. Did she not do this to teach me to have pity on the poor? Certainly what sympathy I have with such owes much to her.

"有一天，她给了我九百文钱，让我送去给她的一位朋友，那人住在城北的'水城'，从南头数第九户人家，面朝河西岸的那条街上。按着这番细致的指引，我找到了狄考文夫人的这位朋友，才发现他极为贫困。不仅如此，他还半身瘫痪；眼睛、嘴巴、手脚都是扭曲的，他四处乞讨为生，行走十分艰难。狄考文夫人竟将这样一个不幸之人视为自己的朋友，还特意差我去探望他。她这样做，岂不正是要教我学会怜恤穷人吗？我如今对这类人所存的怜悯之心，确实大半要归功于她。

" When Mrs. Mateer was on her death-bed, I went to her room for a final leave-taking of my dear teacher. As I knelt to pray by her side, I was overcome with grief. Before I left she took my hand in hers and said, ' I am going to heaven first, and shall wait for you. I hope you will lead a great many people to heaven.' These words sank deep into my heart, and they are a great stimulus to me in my work of soul-saving."

"狄考文夫人临终之时，我来到她房中，向我敬爱的老师作最后的告别。我跪在她身旁祷告时，悲痛难抑。临别前，她握住我的手说：'我要先到天堂去了，会在那里等你。我盼望你能带领许多许多的人到天堂去。'这些话深深地印在我心里，成了我在拯救灵魂这项工作中极大的激励。"

At present Evangelist Ting is going about among the schools and colleges of China, holding revival meetings and enlisting volunteers for the work of the gospel ministry. He is commonly recognized as China's leading evangelist. But Mr. Ting is not the only one marvellously changed and made mighty for God in evangelistic work; for in like manner, through the influence of another great-hearted, deeply pious, and earnest Christian woman, the late Dr. Li, who was formerly a medical student in the government school in Tientsin, became a prominent evangelist in Central China. Such men are the " potency and promise " of the great things that are going to make the world wonder and the angels rejoice, while at the same time they demonstrate the supreme influence cultured Christian women may have in moulding the students of China.

如今，丁布道家奔走于中国各地的中学与大学之间，主领奋兴聚会，并招募志愿者投身福音传道的事工。他被公认为中国首屈一指的布道家。然而，蒙神奇妙改变、在布道事工上大有能力的，并不止丁先生一人；已故的李医生，原本是天津政府学堂的一名医科学生，同样是在另一位心胸宽广、笃信虔诚、热忱恳切的基督徒女性的影响之下，成为了华中地区一位杰出的布道家。这样的人，正是那将使世界惊叹、天使欢欣的伟大事业的"潜能与应许"，同时也证明了有教养的基督徒女性，对塑造中国学子能够产生何等至高的影响力。

Of the graduates of Tengchow College who have gone into business, Liu Shu San is a good example. As a little boy out of a poor family, he entered the Presbyterian Academy at Chefoo, and later went to Tengchow. After graduation he taught in the Chefoo Academy. His salary was not high, but by strict economy he managed to lay by money with which he bought land, building small houses upon it. At that time there was a " boom " in Chefoo, and he, being wise in his investments, accumulated considerable property. Later when T'singtau was opened by the Germans, and a railroad put across the province, he saw that T'singtau was the coming place, and at an early date transferred his investments there, where he is now a prosperous real estate man. He is prominent in business circles and by his exemplary Christian life is a power for good. In various ways he is advancing the Christian cause. Out of his income he has contributed to the college the full amount of his own board and incidental expenses.

在登州文会馆投身商界的毕业生中，刘树森便是一个很好的例子。他出身贫寒，幼年入读烟台的长老会学堂，后来又转到登州求学。毕业后，他在烟台学堂任教。他的薪水并不高，但靠着严格节俭，他攒下了一些钱，用来购置土地，并在其上盖起了小房子。当时烟台正值一波"热潮"，他善于投资理财，积累了不小的产业。后来，德国人开放了青岛，又在省内修建了一条铁路，他看出青岛前途无量，便及早将投资转移到那里，如今已是当地一位颇有成就的房地产商人。他在商界颇有声望，并以身作则的基督徒生活，成为一股向善的力量。他以多种方式推动着基督教的事业。从自己的收入中，他向学院捐出了足以支付自己全部食宿及杂费的款项。

Towards the building of the new church at Chefoo he was a prominent contributor. In T'singtau he himself managed the building of the church and parsonage, contributing largely to them. He was much distressed at seeing the boys from the country falling into sin on coming to the port, so took the lead in organizing a Y. M. C. A., and is now himself paying the whole salary of the secretary. He is also very liberal in helping many needy persons both inside the church and out. A part of his prosperity is due to his great trustworthiness of character, on the strength of which he was able to borrow money at low rate of interest in the beginning of the " boom " at T'singtau and so realize handsomely on his investments.

在烟台新教堂的建造上，他是一位重要的捐助者。在青岛，他亲自主持了教堂和牧师住宅的修建，并为此慷慨解囊。他见到从乡下来到港口的青年们容易堕入罪中，深感忧虑，于是带头组织了基督教青年会，如今更是自己承担着秘书的全部薪水。此外，他对教会内外许多有需要的人，也都十分慷慨地施以援手。他的富足，有一部分要归功于他为人极为可靠的品格——正因如此，青岛"热潮"初起之时，他才得以低息借贷，从而在投资上获得了丰厚的回报。

But although many have thus been engaged in evangelistic and pastoral work, and a few have gone into business, most of the Tengchow graduates have become professors in Christian and government schools in many provinces of China. Four of them are now in chief charge of the government schools in Yunnan, the most southwest of the eighteen provinces. As the hope of China's education must be in Christian teachers, it is desirable that many be prepared in schools similar to Tengchow, and employed by the government in the new education. Such positions bring what in China are large salaries, attended by great temptations. Therefore students must have much personal attention by consecrated workers or they will not stand the strain.

尽管有许多人如此投身于布道和牧养事工，也有少数人从商，但登州文会馆的大多数毕业生，都成了中国各省教会学校及政府学堂的教授。其中四人，如今在云南——十八省中最西南的一省——的政府学堂担任主要负责人。既然中国教育的希望必然寄托于基督徒教师身上，就理当有更多的人在类似登州这样的学校中受到栽培，并受聘于政府投身新式教育。这类职位在中国意味着丰厚的薪金，也伴随着极大的诱惑。因此，学生们必须得到献身的工作者悉心的个人关照，否则将难以承受这种压力。

There follows here one of many life stories of Chinese professors that might be given, illustrating the power of Mrs. Mateer's life and personal touch.

以下便是众多可以列举的中国教授生平故事中的一个，用以说明狄考文夫人的生命及其亲身关怀所具有的感召力。

Sung Fu Wei's mother had been left motherless in her childhood and thus suffered the greatest calamity that can befall a Chinese girl, she had no one to teach her to bind her feet! In fact, like Topsy, she just " growed " and was all her life an unmistakable representative of the " great unwashed." His father was a man of weak character and addicted to gambling. When Sung Fu Wei was a lad of twelve, he and his mother first heard the Gospel preached in their village, and heard it gladly. He soon entered the Tengchow school. Years afterwards when one of his pupils read an essay upon good coming out of evil, Mr. Sung said, " Yes, God can bring good out of even such an evil as gambling. It was gambling that sent me to school. My father gambled and my mother was so afraid I would learn it that when she heard of this school she gave my father no rest until he brought me here." His talents and diligence soon raised him to the head of his class and endeared him to his teachers. When examined for admission to the church and asked why he became a Christian, he replied, " Because Jesus has loved me so much as to die for me."

宋富维的母亲幼年丧母，因此遭遇了一个中国女孩所能遭遇的最大不幸——没有人教她缠足！事实上，她就像（《汤姆叔叔的小屋》里的）托普西一样，只是自顾自地"长大"，一辈子都是不折不扣的"未开化之人"的代表。他的父亲意志薄弱，嗜好赌博。宋富维十二岁那年，他和母亲头一次在村里听到有人传福音，欣然接受。他不久便进了登州学堂。多年以后，一次他的一名学生朗读一篇关于"善能从恶中生出"的作文时，宋先生说道："不错，神甚至能从赌博这样的恶事中带出善来。正是赌博，把我送进了学校。我父亲好赌，我母亲又生怕我沾染上这个毛病，一听说有这所学校，便逼着我父亲不得安宁，非把我送来不可。"他天资聪颖又勤奋好学，很快便成了班上的佼佼者，深得师长喜爱。在接受入教考核、被问及为何成为基督徒时，他答道："因为耶稣如此爱我，甚至为我而死。"

Two years after graduation he returned to Tengchow and was made professor of mathematics, teaching trigonometry, surveying, navigation, analytical geometry and calculus, and teaching them well. He was Dr. Mateer's most efficient helper in preparing his mathematical series for the press, being not only well versed in the subject as taught in western lands, but having bestowed much labor on the Chinese mathematical works.

毕业两年后，他回到登州，被聘为数学教授，教授三角学、测量学、航海学、解析几何和微积分，且教得极好。他是狄考文博士编写数学丛书付印过程中最得力的助手，不仅精通西方所授的这门学问，还在中国的数学典籍上下过不少功夫。

Mr. Sung was not more distinguished for his talents and attainments than for his good judgment, his faithfulness as a teacher, his earnest piety, and the real missionary spirit in all his work. When only twenty-three years old he was elected an elder in the church and maintained in high degree the respect and confidence of all. He often led the weekly prayer-meeting, and in the absence of his pastor sometimes led the Sunday services. His addresses and sermons were always clear, logical, Scriptural and helpful, and showed a deep work of grace in his heart.

宋先生的才华与学识固然出众，但他良好的判断力、作为教师的忠信、恳切的敬虔，以及贯穿于他一切工作中真正的宣教精神，同样为人称道。年仅二十三岁时，他便被选为教会长老，并始终深得众人的敬重与信任。他常常主领每周的祷告会，牧师不在时，有时也主领主日崇拜。他的讲论和讲道，向来条理清晰、合乎圣经、切实有益，显明他心中深厚的恩典之工。

Notwithstanding his ability he was quiet and unassuming. He once told his pupils how very large he felt when as a lad he went home for a vacation after passing his first examination in arithmetic. Said he: " All my friends were wondering at my knowledge of mathematics, and I thought myself about the wisest man in the country. Here I have been studying and teaching mathematics ever since, and only learn better every year how much there is I do not know."

尽管才干出众，他却为人低调谦逊。他曾告诉学生们，自己少年时头一次算术考试通过、放假回家时，是何等的自命不凡。他说："我所有的朋友都惊叹于我的数学知识，我还以为自己差不多是国内最有学问的人了。此后我一直研习并教授数学，却是年复一年才愈发明白，自己所不知道的东西究竟有多少。"

He valued highly his school privileges. He was accustomed to say that Christian boarding schools in heathen lands advance the cause of Christ by at least three or four generations; first, by raising up reliable men and women as helpers in building up the church, and second, by setting an example of the Christian training of children. Said he, " It is not enough that we see how the missionaries train their children; we need to see the effects of Christian training on Chinese children."

他极为珍视自己所受的教育。他常说，在异教之地设立的基督教寄宿学校，至少能使基督的事业向前推进三四代人之久；一方面，是因为它培育出了可靠的男女人才，作为建立教会的助手；另一方面，则是为基督化教养儿女树立了榜样。他说："光看宣教士如何教养自己的儿女是不够的；我们还需要看到基督化教养在中国孩子身上所结出的果效。"

After all, the most admirable trait in Mr. Sung's character was his patient endurance of trials such as fall to the lot of few men. His mother's untidy house and peculiar ways were always a source of mortification to him, and her years of ill health a cause of constant anxiety; but he had the comfort of seeing in her much evidence that she was a child of God. His father was for years halting between the church and the gambling table, at which he finally wasted the whole of his little property. He had reformed and fallen so often that when he died after a year and a half of good behavior and apparently sincere repentance, his son could not but feel a deep and grateful sense of relief. Notwithstanding their faults, Mr. Sung's conduct towards his parents and his words about them always exemplified the spirit of the command, " Honor thy father and thy mother."

归根结底，宋先生品格中最令人钦佩之处，是他对种种试炼所表现出的忍耐——那是极少有人会遭遇的试炼。他母亲那杂乱无章的家和古怪的行事作风，始终是令他难堪的源头，她多年的病弱之身，也是他持续忧心的缘由；但令他欣慰的是，他能从母亲身上看到诸多证据，证明她确实是神的儿女。他的父亲多年来在教会与赌桌之间摇摆不定，最终把家中仅有的一点产业挥霍殆尽。他屡次悔改，又屡次故态复萌，以至于当他在一年半的良好操守和看似真诚的悔改之后离世时，儿子心中除了深切的哀伤，竟也难免生出一份感恩的释然。尽管父母有种种过失，宋先生待他们的行为举止和谈及他们的言辞，却始终活出了"当孝敬父母"这条诫命的精神。

While yet a schoolboy, he brought his only sister to school and provided her clothing. Besides carrying on his own studies, he earned the money to do this and also to clothe himself, by teaching algebra and geometry in the Baptist school. Shortly after graduation he married a Christian Chinese girl of great personal attractions. Her health soon became seriously impaired and she continued to be a great sufferer. This was blessed to her growth in some graces, but made her irritable and exacting, and she was always selfish and extravagant. Her husband's income never exceeded seventy dollars a year. On her account he denied himself everything but the barest necessities of life. He never neglected his wife for his work, and he never neglected his work for his wife, but spent himself freely for both. His mind was always busy with work or plans for his pupils or in self-improvement for their sakes, and his heart always burdened with solicitude for his poor wife and sympathy with her sufferings, which the most assiduous medical attention failed to remove; she was neither brave nor thoughtful enough to conceal what he was powerless to relieve.

还在读书的时候，他就把自己唯一的妹妹带来上学，并供她衣着所需。除了自己的学业之外，他还靠在浸信会学校教授代数和几何，挣钱供妹妹和自己所需的费用。毕业不久，他娶了一位容貌出众、信仰虔诚的中国女子为妻。她的健康不久便每况愈下，此后长年饱受病痛之苦。这使她在某些美德上有所长进，却也让她变得易怒挑剔，且始终自私、挥霍无度。她丈夫的收入从未超过每年七十块银元。为了妻子，他自己的生活除了最基本的必需之外，什么都舍弃了。他从未因工作而疏忽妻子，也从未因妻子而疏忽工作，而是心甘情愿地为两者尽心竭力。他的心思，总是忙于工作，或是为学生谋划，或是为学生的缘故不断自我提升；而他的心，也总是为体弱的妻子担忧牵挂，同情她的病痛——即便最尽心的医疗照顾，也未能使这病痛得到缓解；而她既不够坚强，也不够体贴，无法掩藏那份他自己却无力纾解的痛苦。

No one ever heard him complain of his burdens or saw him shed tears over his own griefs. He had tears for others' sorrows but not for his own. When, during a very unhealthy season, he was smitten by dysentery, that deadly foe to human life in Tengchow, he trusted to his vigorous constitution, which had never before known a severe illness, to throw it off. True to his instincts he was too busy for others to take thought of himself till the disease had taken such a hold on him that the most careful attention of his physician and care of his friends availed nothing to check its course. One short, sharp conflict, and his toils and trials were ended forever. The good and faithful servant entered into the joy of his Lord.

从没有人听他抱怨过自己肩上的重担，也没有人见他为自己的哀伤流过一滴眼泪。他会为别人的忧愁落泪，却从不为自己而落泪。在一个极不利于健康的季节，他染上了痢疾——这是登州夺人性命的一大凶疾——他仗着自己一向强健、从未患过重病的体质，以为能够将病挺过去。他一如既往，忙于他人之事，无暇顾及自己，直到病势沉重到即便医生最悉心的诊治、朋友们最周到的照料，都已无力回天。一场短暂而剧烈的搏斗之后，他一生的劳苦与试炼便永远画上了句号。这位又良善又忠心的仆人，终于进入了他主人的喜乐。

Dr. Mills, his pastor, says in connection with his death: " In some of the highest manifestations of mental power, the Chinese are fully our equals. When thoroughly controlled by Christian principle they are quite as disinterested as we are. Unemotional as they seem to us, they are capable of the rarest self-sacrifice." It is worthy of remark that Christianity could bestow within the compass of a single life, and that the first generation removed from heathenism, not only such Christlike character, but the changed, considerate, and even chivalrous attitude towards mother and sister and delicate wife, which is among the finest fruits of Christian civilization.

他的牧师米尔斯博士，在谈及他的离世时说道："在心智才能最高的一些表现上，中国人完全可以与我们比肩。当他们真正受基督教原则的掌管时，他们的无私程度，也丝毫不亚于我们。尽管在我们看来，他们似乎不动声色，但他们所能做出的自我牺牲，却是极为罕见的。"值得一提的是，基督教竟能在短短一生之内、在脱离异教信仰仅仅一代人的时间里，不仅塑造出这般效法基督的品格，还塑造出对待母亲、姐妹以及体弱之妻那般已然改变、体贴入微、甚至堪称侠义的态度——这正是基督教文明所结出的最美好的果实之一。

第八章 · Chapter 8 医疗工作与影响 Medical Work and Influence

A GREAT deal of medical work devolved upon Mrs. Mateer. This was partly an unwelcome necessity. There were many years when the station had no missionary doctor and the Chinese doctors were little better than none. Those who live in Christian countries fail to appreciate that everything worth while in their civilization is a by-product of the Gospel. In heathen countries, there is no relief even from toothache. One missionary was in the habit of pulling a lot of teeth upon entering a village and thus getting the people in good humor for the hearing of the Gospel.

大量的医疗工作，都落在了狄考文夫人肩上。这在某种程度上，是她并不情愿、却又不得不承担的责任。有许多年，差会驻地都没有宣教医生，而中国的郎中也几乎与没有相差无几。生活在基督教国家的人，很难体会到他们文明中一切值得称道之处，其实都是福音所结出的副产品。在异教之地，就连牙痛都无处求医。有一位宣教士就习惯于每到一处村庄，便先给人拔上几颗牙，借此让村民心情愉悦，再来听福音。

Mrs. Mateer's Christian sympathies could not resist the many appeals for relief from suffering. She came to consider what she did in this way as an effective means for influencing people in a spiritual way. Mrs. Fanny Corbett Hays says: " Never lived a missionary who more lovingly and conscientiously entered into the details of the common daily life of the people who pressed upon her. Who that has seen her, as she every morning met the ailing and sick students in her little medicine closet under the stairs, can ever forget the sight? She had great skill in diagnosing and treating the simple ailments of the boys, who came on an average of ten a day, and she prevented many a serious illness by her timely remedies. But she told me that she did not reach her highest purpose in her little dispensary when she cured the diseases of the body, being more concerned to treat the homesick boy, to detect sorrow and repentance, and to give medicine for the soul."

狄考文夫人天生的基督徒同情心，使她无法拒绝那许许多多恳求解除病痛的呼声。她渐渐认识到，以这样的方式行事，正是在属灵层面影响百姓的一个有效途径。范妮·郭显德·赫士夫人（Mrs. Fanny Corbett Hays）说："从未有哪位宣教士，像她这样满怀爱心、一丝不苟地投入到那些向她涌来的百姓的日常琐事之中。凡是见过她的人，看她每天清晨在楼梯下那间小小的药房里接待前来求诊的患病学生，又怎能忘怀那情景？她极擅长诊断和治疗男孩们的一般小病，他们平均每天要来十人，而她也靠着及时的诊治，防患于未然，避免了许多严重的疾病。但她告诉我，仅仅治好了身体上的疾病，还不是她在这间小小诊所里所追求的最高目标；她更关心的，是抚慰那想家的孩子，察觉他心中的忧伤与悔意，为他的灵魂开出药方。"

In the long and severe illnesses of the students, she prescribed for and nursed them with all the tender care of a mother. She prepared their food and saw that it was given at the proper time. She was never too weary nor the hour too late to see a sick pupil. A former Tengchow boy writes as follows:

学生们患上长期而严重的疾病时，她总是以母亲般的温柔悉心照料，为他们诊治、护理。她亲自为他们备餐，留意按时进食。无论多么疲惫，无论时辰多晚，她从不因此而不去探望患病的学生。一位登州的老校友这样写道：

" Whenever I was sick, Mrs. Mateer spent much time and heart upon me. After graduation I went to a distant place leaving my wife and son at Tengchow. When the cholera was raging there an old man who lived in the same yard with my family had one day buried twenty cholera victims. When he came home in the evening my wife prepared him some food. My son took the cholera from him. His mother was in great distress and sent the old man for Mrs. Mateer. She and Dr. Mateer were at prayers, just before retiring. She came quickly and worked all night with the boy. In the early dawn he was better and gradually recovered. Recently he has passed successfully the examination for students going to America at government expense. Such kindness we can never forget." (This he wrote with tears streaming down his face.)

"每逢我生病，狄考文夫人总要花许多的时间和心力照顾我。毕业后，我去了外地任职，把妻子和儿子留在了登州。那里霍乱肆虐之时，与我家同住一个院子的一位老人，一天之内就埋葬了二十个霍乱死者。他傍晚回家，我妻子给他做了些吃食。我的儿子因此染上了霍乱。他母亲心急如焚，打发那老人去请狄考文夫人。她和狄考文博士当时正在做临睡前的祷告。她立刻赶来，整夜守着这孩子。到了黎明，孩子渐渐好转，终于痊愈。近来他成功通过了公费留美学生的考试。这样的恩情，我们永远不能忘怀。"（他写下这段话时，泪水顺着脸颊流淌不止。）

" Mrs. Mateer had this same interest in all her boys. Whenever any one was absent from prayer-meeting or church she was concerned lest he might be sick and went at once to see. She cared also for the sick and the poor not only among the Christians, but the non-Christians as well. My wife in going around preaching found an old couple in dire distress with no one to care for them. When Mrs. Mateer heard of them she gave them a thousand cash, bought a coat for the old man and some grain. Later she gave them money to use as capital in buying grain to grind and sell so that they might help themselves. When another old man who sold sweet potatoes had a serious sore on his leg, Mrs. Mateer repeatedly gave him medicine and money. These are but a few of her manifold kindnesses."

"狄考文夫人对她所有的孩子，都怀着同样的关切。每当有人缺席祷告会或礼拜，她便担心他是不是病了，立刻前去探望。她所照顾的病人和穷人，不仅限于基督徒，也包括非信徒。我妻子外出布道时，发现一对老夫妇处境凄惨，无人照料。狄考文夫人听闻后，给了他们一千文钱，为老人买了一件外衣和一些粮食。后来，她又给了他们一笔钱作本钱，让他们能买些粮食磨了卖，以自食其力。另有一位卖红薯的老人，腿上生了严重的疮，狄考文夫人也一再给他送药送钱。这些不过是她诸多善行中的寥寥几例。"

Below is given a single instance of her experiences in the medical work. She says: " This morning a little boy, to whom I gave some eye-water a few days ago and who evidently considered that a sufficient introduction, came to the door. His hair was neatly brushed and his hands and clothes were clean. He was sent, he said, to ask the lady to go see a sick person in the west suburb. I decided at once to go, as it is a neighborhood to which we have not hitherto had access. I found the patient to be a young woman suffering from scrofula. The neighbors soon began crowding in so that not only the house but the court was filled, and numerous were the applications for medicine.

以下便是她行医经历中的一个例子。她说："今天早上，一个小男孩来到门口，几天前我给他滴过一点眼药水，他显然觉得这已经足以算作相识了。他头发梳得整整齐齐，手和衣服也是干干净净的。他说，他是奉命来请这位女士去西郊看一个病人的。我当即决定前去，因为那是我们以往从未能进入的一片街区。我发现病人是个患了瘰疬的年轻女子。邻居们很快便围拢过来，不仅屋里挤满了人，连院子也站得满满当当，前来讨药的人络绎不绝。

" After disposing of such cases as well as I could, I began to preach or talk 'doctrine,' as we say here. I began about the Heavenly Ruler, whom they worship once every year, and who is the true God as far as they have retained any knowledge of Him; but I failed to secure any one's attention. Then I began about the soul, by asking what becomes of it after death, but with no better success, though they are usually curious on this point. I also tried the story of our first parents, and the worship of idols, but still without any success, though I have scarcely ever before known a case where one or the other of the last two subjects would not for a while, at least, secure attention. No one would listen to anything but my answers to their numerous questions about the 'foreign country,' as every place outside of China is called. I left, greatly discouraged by the fruitlessness of my visit, but with a silent prayer that after all it might not be quite in vain.

"尽可能处理完这些病例之后，我便开始讲道，或者按我们这里的说法，'讲讲教义'。我从他们每年祭拜一次的天帝讲起——就他们残存的一点认知而言，那便是真神；但我没能引起任何人的注意。接着我讲到灵魂，问他们人死后灵魂会往何处去，结果也不见得更好，尽管他们通常对这个话题颇感好奇。我又试着讲我们人类始祖的故事，以及拜偶像之事，仍是一无所获——但以往我几乎从未遇到过，讲这后两个话题竟完全无法哪怕一时引起注意的情形。他们什么都不肯听，只肯听我回答他们关于'外国'——他们这样称呼中国以外的一切地方——的种种问题。我离开时，因这次拜访毫无成效而深感沮丧，只是默默祷告，盼望这一切终究不至于全然徒劳。

" I had not gone far, when I heard some one call, and looking back saw an elderly woman follow me. She said she wanted to ask about the true God and how to worship Him. We sat down by a well. I remembered how my Saviour had sat by the well and talked of the 'water of life.' She told me much that she had heard from Mrs. Crawford of the Baptist Mission, and then, folding her hands and bowing her head, she repeated what she could remember of a little prayer.

"我还没走多远，就听见有人喊我，回头一看，是一位上了年纪的妇人跟了上来。她说她想问问真神的事，以及该如何敬拜祂。我们便在一口井边坐下。我想起我的救主也曾坐在井边，谈论'生命的活水'。她告诉我许多她从浸信会克劳福德夫人（Mrs. Crawford）那里听来的话，然后，双手合十、低下头，念起了她记得的一小段祷文。

She added, ' I am an old woman and have time to learn of this "doctrine," and I think if there is any way for my soul to go to heaven when I die, it will be a very good thing, so I wish to learn it. Unfortunately, I have forgotten much that Mrs. Crawford told me. If you will just write out some directions for me, and a prayer, then I could get some one to read to me when I forget.' Her idea evidently was that there were certain things for her to do, and a prayer she must repeat a certain number of times, and she would be saved. Great as her mistake was, I was delighted to find a woman who having heard only once knew even this much, and cared to know more. I explained to her something of the nature of prayer, and of our obligations to God, and promised her a written prayer and a book. She promised to come to see me soon and bring her sister with her.

她又说：'我年纪大了，有的是时间学这"教义"，我想，我死后灵魂若真有办法能上天堂，那实在是件极好的事，所以我愿意学。只可惜，克劳福德夫人教我的许多东西我都忘了。您若能给我写下一些法子，再写一段祷文，等我忘了的时候，也好找人念给我听。'她显然以为，只要做某些特定的事，把某段祷文念上特定的遍数，便可以得救。尽管她的想法大有偏差，我还是很高兴看到，一个只听过一次道的妇人，竟能记得这么多，并且还愿意知道更多。我向她解释了祷告的本质，以及我们对神当尽的本分，并答应给她写一段祷文、送她一本书。她答应不久后会来看我，并带上她的妹妹一起来。

" While we were talking, a ragged, dirty woman, carrying a sick child, came out and begged me to go to a house near by where all the family were sick of typhoid fever. They had neither food nor medicine and no money. They lived in an old stone hut which stands in a miserably dirty court, its clay floor being more than a foot lower than the ground outside and its walls and ceiling blackened by the smoke of many years. The one door was broken off its hinges and lay in the court.

"我们正说着话，一个衣衫褴褛、浑身脏污的妇人，抱着一个生病的孩子走出来，求我去附近一户人家，说那家人全都染上了伤寒。他们既无食物也无药，也没有钱。他们住在一间破旧的石屋里，坐落在一个肮脏不堪的院子中，屋内的泥地比屋外的地面还要低出一尺多，墙壁和屋顶都被多年的烟熏得漆黑。唯一的一扇门，也已从合页上脱落，倒在院子里。

" Making my way through the dirt and rubbish of the outer room, I looked into the sick-room. Oh, what a sight! On their kang (brick bed), about seven feet long by three wide, lay a man, his wife, two boys nine and eleven years old, all in a high fever, and an infant two days old. There they had lain for days, in mid-summer. The single small window was papered so as to exclude every particle of air and light. The woman was in great distress and somewhat delirious. A miserable beggar woman, hired for about two cents a day, was the only person who went near them. I asked if there were no neighbors who would take the baby home and care for it until the mother was able to do so. The man answered with an astonished look, ' We have no such custom!'

"我穿过外屋的污秽和杂物，望进那间病房。啊，好一副景象！在一张约七英尺长、三英尺宽的炕上，躺着一个男人、他的妻子、两个九岁和十一岁的男孩，全都发着高烧，还有一个刚出生两天的婴儿。他们就这样躺了好几天，正值仲夏时节。唯一的一扇小窗被纸糊得严严实实，一丝空气和光线都透不进来。那妇人痛苦不堪，神志有些不清。唯一靠近他们的人，是一个每天雇价约两分钱的可怜乞讨妇人。我问是否没有邻居愿意把婴儿接去照料，直到母亲能够照顾他为止。那男人一脸诧异地答道：'我们没有这种规矩！'

" I could not restrain my indignation that, among a people who pretend to such high civilization, no one could be found to show kindness to people in such distress, because they were of the lowest and poorest class. In all this great city and among many relatives, neighbors and friends, they had none. I hurried home to procure some food and medicine which I sent them and this evening the mother is quietly sleeping, and the others less miserable.

"我实在按捺不住心中的愤慨——在这样一个自诩文明程度极高的民族当中，竟找不到一个人肯对身陷如此困境的人施以援手，只因为他们出身最卑贱、最贫穷的阶层。在这偌大的城中，尽管亲友邻里众多，他们却举目无亲。我匆匆赶回家，取了些食物和药品送去，到了今晚，那母亲已能安睡，其余的人也不那么痛苦了。

" When starting home my tidy friend of the morning came running after me, and insisted on escorting me home. He had divested himself of every stitch of clothing, and though his hands and face were still not very dirty, it must have been months since the rest of him was in clean water. It is scarcely necessary to say that I declined his offer." Throughout the years such experiences as the above were of common occurrence.

"我动身回家时，早上那位干净整洁的小朋友追了上来，坚持要送我回去。他已经把身上唯一的一件衣服都脱掉了，虽然手和脸看着还不算很脏，但身体其他部位大概已经好几个月没沾过干净水了。我婉拒了他的好意，这自不必多说。"多年以来，诸如此类的经历，可谓司空见惯。

Mrs. Mateer's medical work during the famine of 1878 was somewhat special. " Since the spring opened we have been much occupied with the famine sufferers. No day passes without calls for food and medicine, and some days are entirely filled with the work. I began to keep a list of the principal cases but stopped at the fourteenth of regular typhoid fever. It was too much trouble to keep the record. I suppose that at least sixty fever cases owe to me all the medicine they got, and to my directions all the nursing. One of the first symptoms in every case was nausea, and usually the preparatory medicine required was a dose of santonin, with oil and lard and laudanum. This involved not only measuring out the medicine, but finding a cup to put it in, securing its return, making a mustard plaster to keep the medicine down till it had time to take effect, providing disinfectants and seeing that they were used, sending food for the patient and fuel with which to cook it.

1878年饥荒期间，狄考文夫人的医疗工作尤为特殊。"自春天以来，我们大半的精力都用在了照顾饥民身上。几乎没有一天不接到求食求药的呼求，有些日子更是被这工作占得满满当当。我原本开始记录主要的病例，但记到第十四例典型的伤寒病人时，便停下了——记录实在太麻烦了。我估计至少有六十例热病病人，所服的药全都出自我手，护理也全靠我的指导。每个病例最初的症状之一都是恶心，通常需要先服一剂驱虫药，再配上油、猪油和鸦片酊。这不仅意味着要量取药物，还得找来一只杯子盛药、想法要回杯子、做一副芥末膏药来固住药力使其见效、备好消毒药水并督促使用、还要给病人送去食物，以及用来煮食的柴火。

" One man came with two dreadful sores. They had opened and should have been discharging freely, but according to their custom he had plastered them over with a stiff, sticky plaster and was suffering intensely. I gave him a basin of warm water and a sponge with which he succeeded in about half an hour in getting them reasonably cleaned off, revealing four great patches of proud flesh. I poulticed with bread and told the man he must change the poultice in the evening. Fearing he would not do so I offered him the bread. He said he was sleeping with a friend and had not the face to have anything done for him. Said I, ' You have not the face to ask your friend to make a poultice, but you can come here and ask the "foreign devils" for anything you want?' ' Exactly so,' said he, with the utmost simplicity. Said I, ' Come back this evening and I will make you fresh poultices.' In two days he was sufficiently relieved to go to Chefoo to a physician.

"有个男人身上带着两处骇人的疮口来求诊。疮口已经溃破，本该畅快地流脓，但按照他们的习俗，他却用一种又硬又粘的膏药糊了上去，痛苦不堪。我给了他一盆温水和一块海绵，他花了大约半小时，才勉强把伤口清理干净，露出了四大片腐肉。我用面包给他做了敷料，并叮嘱他晚上一定要换药。担心他不肯照办，我便把面包直接给了他。他说自己是借宿在朋友家，没脸让人为自己费这个心思。我说：'你没脸求朋友给你做敷料，倒有脸跑到这里来向"洋鬼子"要东要西？''正是如此，'他答得极为坦然。我说：'今晚再来，我给你换新的敷料。'两天后，他的伤势已大为好转，足以前往烟台去看医生了。

" Tso Li Wen, our new teacher, plays surgeon for me and when we both get beyond our depth, we call on Mr. Mateer to help us out. Of my fever patients, one old woman and one little girl died. One man brought a basket of eggs and cherries to express his gratitude. Another waylaid me in the street, as I came from prayer-meeting one dark night, to thank me. The women have been profuse in their thanks. If I were a physician, perhaps I should find pleasure in this work, but of all things I do dislike to give medicine. I do not like to handle it, and as I can rarely feel very sure I am doing the best thing, it worries me exceedingly. Sometimes I am so thankful we can relieve these poor sufferers, and sometimes I wish we never had the means of giving a single dose, because then I could turn away all such cases with a clear conscience. This is my plea for the woman physician who is to be sent out this autumn."

"我们的新教习左立文（Tso Li Wen）替我充当外科大夫，遇到我们俩都束手无策的时候，就去请狄考文先生来帮忙。我所诊治的热病病人中，一位老妇人和一个小女孩不幸去世了。一个男人带了一篮鸡蛋和樱桃来表达谢意。还有一个人，在一个漆黑的夜晚，趁我从祷告会回来的路上拦住我，向我致谢。妇女们更是感激不尽。若我是个正儿八经的医生，或许还能从这工作中寻得几分乐趣，但世上我最不喜欢的事，莫过于给人开药。我不喜欢摆弄药物，而且我很少能十分确信自己所做的就是最妥当的处置，这让我极为忧心。有时我万分感恩，我们竟能救助这些可怜的病患；但有时我又宁愿我们从不曾有能力施舍哪怕一剂药，因为那样一来，我便可以问心无愧地回绝所有这类求助了。这也正是我恳求今秋派来一位女医生的缘由。"

Mrs. Mateer's experience emphasizes the precious results to be obtained from seeing that patients treated are followed up in a spiritual way after their return home. The story of Mrs. Li, a most intelligent and interesting old woman, may be given to illustrate how faithfully she herself sought to take advantage of interest awakened through the medical work.

狄考文夫人的经历，凸显了一点极为宝贵的成效——病人接受治疗、返回家中之后，若能在属灵方面持续跟进，便能结出可贵的果子。李夫人的故事，正可以用来说明，她自己是何等忠心地把握住医疗工作所唤起的这份兴趣。这位李夫人是一位极为聪慧、饶有意趣的老妇人。

" Mrs. Li first came here one Sabbath morning to see whether we could do anything for her daughter whose arm and hand had been dreadfully scalded. The daughter lived with the mother-in-law, who was very unkind to her. She was subject to fits of hysteria, and in one of these she had fallen into a kettle of boiling rice. Mr. Mateer made up a wash and gave to her, although he had very little hope of being able to effect a cure. Soon after this, the mother took her daughter home to herself, and I went to see her. She had used the wash most faithfully and in a few days more allowed Mr. Mateer to go once to see her. He afterwards made her an ointment which I took her, showing her how to wash and dress the hand. She followed directions very carefully, and in two months was quite well. They were grateful to us, and as the best expression they could give of their gratitude, according to native custom, the young woman wished to be my adopted daughter — not Mr. Mateer's, that would have been immodest.

"李夫人头一次来，是在一个礼拜日的早晨，来问我们能否为她女儿的手臂和手做点什么——那手臂和手被严重烫伤了。她女儿和婆婆住在一起，婆婆待她十分刻薄。她时常犯歇斯底里的病症，有一次发作时，跌进了一锅正煮着的滚烫米饭中。狄考文先生配了一种洗剂给她，虽然他自己也不抱多大希望能够治好。此后不久，她母亲把女儿接回了自己家中，我便前去探望。她极为忠实地使用了那洗剂，几天后，又准许狄考文先生再去看她一次。他后来又配制了一种药膏，由我带去给她，并教她如何清洗和包扎那只手。她十分谨慎地遵照指示行事，两个月后便痊愈了。他们对我们感激不尽，按照当地的习俗，为了表达他们所能想到的最高谢意，这位年轻女子希望能做我的义女——而不是狄考文先生的义女，那样便不够端庄了。

" Both Mrs. Holmes and I felt a deep interest in the old woman from the first, and were anxious to arouse her to the importance of the doctrines we teach; but suddenly she ceased to come, and we feared we had lost all trace of her. After three months she appeared again, saying that they had moved to a distant part of the city, that they had not forgotten us, but she had been ill, and her daughter was too young (about thirty) to be seen on the streets alone. She made a long visit, listened very attentively to all I told her, and quite encouraged me by saying she had never gone to the temples to pray for her daughter since Mrs. Holmes told her those were all false gods. Instead, she had prayed every day to the true God of whom we had told her. All her prayer, however, had been for her daughter's recovery. She was very self-righteous, was sure she loved God and would keep His law, and promised at once to observe the Sabbath. I explained to her most of the commandments, and she left us, sure of working out her own salvation. She and her daughter-in-law, who lives with her, are poor, earning their living by sewing, and it means genuine sacrifice that they both keep the Sabbath.

"霍姆斯夫人（Mrs. Holmes）和我，一开始便对这位老妇人深感兴趣，都渴望能唤起她对我们所传教义之重要性的认识；但她忽然不再来了，我们担心从此失去了她的音讯。三个月后，她又出现了，说他们搬到了城中较远的地方，并没有忘记我们，只是她自己病了，女儿年纪又太轻（约三十岁），不便独自上街抛头露面。她这次待了很久，十分专注地听我讲的一切，还让我颇感欣慰地说，自从霍姆斯夫人告诉她那些都是假神以后，她就再也没去过庙里为女儿祈祷，而是改为每天向我们所讲的真神祷告。不过，她所有的祷告，都是为了女儿康复。她自以为义的心思很重，坚信自己爱神、会遵守祂的律法，还当即应许要守安息日。我向她讲解了大部分诫命，她便告辞而去，深信自己能够凭一己之力成就救恩。她和与她同住的儿媳，都靠做针线活为生，家境贫寒，两人都能坚持守安息日，实在是一份真切的牺牲。

" Afterwards Mrs. Li began to come Sunday afternoons for instruction, and she was seldom absent. She would not consent to go in the morning to the chapel, because some relative of her deceased husband lived near, whom Chinese custom says she must never allow to see her. She reads the Mandarin pretty well, has several books which she says she reads every day, praying morning and evening and trying to teach her children as much as she can. I cannot go to see her where she now lives because the landlord will not allow foreigners to visit her. Sometimes after a long conversation I hope she has indeed passed from death unto life; at other times, I am afraid she has not the root of the matter in her.

"此后，李夫人开始每逢主日下午来听道，鲜少缺席。她却不肯在早上去礼拜堂，因为附近住着她已故丈夫的一位亲戚，按中国的规矩，她绝不能让对方看见自己。她官话读得相当不错，她说自己有几本书，每天都读，早晚祷告，并尽力教导她的孩子们。她如今所住之处，我无法前去探望，因为房东不许外国人登门造访。有时，经过一番长谈之后，我盼望她确实已经出死入生；但有时，我又担心她心中并未真正扎下这道理的根。

" It has been most interesting to see her self-confidence giving way. The process has been a very gradual one. Sometimes I feared she was making no progress, but on looking back several weeks there was evident change. The matter of forgiving her daughter's mother-in-law was very difficult. When I told her she must do this, she said, ' No, I will not.' I referred her to Christ's example, but she answered, ' Ah, yes! He was God, and I am not.' However, at last she was convinced it was her duty to forgive and pray for her, and she has manifested since quite a different disposition towards her.

"看着她那份自负渐渐消退，实在是件极有意思的事。这个过程极为缓慢。有时我担心她毫无长进，但回想几周前的光景，变化其实已十分明显。要她饶恕女儿的婆婆，是一件极难的事。我告诉她必须这样做时，她说：'不，我不肯。'我便以基督的榜样开导她，她却答道：'唉，话是不错！可祂是神，我不是。'不过，最终她还是明白了，饶恕并为对方祷告是她的本分，此后她对那婆婆的态度也确实大不相同了。

" I have not had my sympathies so thoroughly enlisted for any other Chinese woman; indeed, I have not met among the Chinese women any other so intelligent, or so interesting a character."

"我从未对哪个中国妇女倾注过如此深切的关怀；说实话，在中国妇女中，我也从未遇到过第二个如此聪慧、如此引人入胜的人物。"

This old lady, Mrs. Li, became a devoted Christian, and was faithful to the end. Not long before Mrs. Mateer's death, she passed away at the ripe old age of eighty years.

这位李夫人后来成了一名虔诚的基督徒，至死信心坚定。就在狄考文夫人去世前不久，她也以八十高龄安然辞世。

第九章 · Chapter 9 巡行布道 Itinerating

MRS. MATEER itinerated extensively and persistently. She accompanied her husband on most of his itinerating tours and also took many trips alone, or in company with other women of the station, going to distances ranging from fifty to two hundred and twenty miles, and spending in time from two weeks to three months. She travelled a great deal on donkeys on a Chinese pack-saddle. She took at least one tour each year until the last year, and frequently two or more. She visited, in this way, the homes of all the Chinese Christians connected with Tengchow station, and many connected with Chefoo and Weihsien, exhorting, teaching, and praying with the women and children. She made a special point of visiting the homes of her pupils, older and younger, and no pupil even from a heathen home ever failed to make a warm welcome for her from his father and mother.

狄考文夫人巡行布道，既广泛又持之以恒。她大多数时候都陪同丈夫一同外出巡行，此外还常常独自出行，或与差会驻地其他女性同工结伴，行程远至五十英里到二百二十英里不等，每次外出所需的时日，从两周到三个月不等。她大量的行程，都是骑着驴子、用中式驮鞍代步。除了最后一年，她每年至少要出行一次，往往还不止一两次。她就是以这样的方式，探访了所有与登州差会有关联的中国基督徒的家庭，也探访了许多与烟台、潍县有关的家庭，向妇女和孩童劝勉、教导，并与她们一同祷告。她尤其注重探访学生们的家庭，无论年长年幼，即便是来自异教家庭的学生，其父母也从未有一次不热情地欢迎她的到来。

She dearly loved to preach the Gospel. A visiting friend says: " One evening Dr. Mateer and I went into the parlor after an intelligent Chinese who had been with her several hours had departed. Mrs. Mateer was sitting in a large chair with her face shining with the light of God. Its radiance startled me, as she put her hands up and pushed the hair back from her temples saying, ' Oh, I do love to preach!' Her husband looked at her with a tender, comprehending smile, as she added, ' I wish I could devote my whole time to preaching. I have so many cares and have to do so many other things, when I would so much rather preach.'"

她深深热爱传讲福音。一位来访的朋友说："一天晚上，一位见识不凡的中国人在她那里坐了好几个小时才离去，狄考文博士和我随后走进客厅。只见狄考文夫人坐在一张大椅子上，脸上闪耀着神的光辉。那光彩令我为之一震，她一边用手把两鬓的头发向后拢去，一边说：'哦，我实在是太爱传道了！'她丈夫望着她，报以一个温柔而会心的微笑，她又补充道：'我真希望能把全部的时间都用来传道。我有太多的杂事要操心，还有太多别的事情要做，可我却是多么愿意传道啊。'"

This passion, together with her loving sympathy and concern for heathen women and girls, often led her out to the country.
