The Board proposes that I have two-thirds of the vacation, which is clearly illegal. Fifth, Mr. Mateer's work is already as much as he can do in justice to himself. I think it would be ungenerous, after all these years of labor, to lay upon him such additional burdens as my absence would involve. Certainly, we must have clearer light before I will consent either to leave the school without a competent superintendent, or to leave my husband to bear the burden of it alone." We may fairly infer from some of her letters that for personal reasons, also, she was loath to leave her husband for so long a time, and this, no doubt, unconsciously weighed with her.

对于差会理事会提议让狄考文夫人提前回国一事，她表示了异议，说道："第一，中国人向来认为，探望母亲是狄考文先生的本分。事实上，按照他们对孝道与夫妇之义的理解，若我们二人不能同时回去，我理应等丈夫先回去、再回来之后，我才好动身；否则，若我先走一步，恐怕会动摇教会的信心。第二，我这个'病人'看起来实在太健康了，我担心，若我这样长时间地回国休养，反倒会成为教会的一块绊脚石。第三，除非确有必要，否则我不能被抽调走整整两年，而这份必要性，非得亲身经历才能证明。第四，我们打算休假一年半，也就是说，我们两人合计需要三年。狄考文先生和我一样，确实需要这么长的时间。按规矩，妻子只能休三分之一的假期。而理事会却提议让我占去休假的三分之二，这显然是不合规的。第五，狄考文先生手头的工作，本已是他一人分内所能承受的极限。我想，在他辛劳了这么多年之后，若因我离去而给他额外增添这样的重担，未免太过不近人情。总之，除非我们能看得更清楚，否则我是不会同意的——无论是撇下学校、任其无人妥善督管，还是撇下丈夫、让他独自一人承担这份重担。"从她的一些书信中，我们也不难推断，出于个人的原因，她同样不愿与丈夫分离这么长的时间，这份心思，想必也在不知不觉中影响了她的决定。

When in Chefoo in search of health she says: " I have regular fits of homesickness every Wednesday when the messenger comes from Tengchow, and in spite of all my advantage, am only getting weaker and thinner every week. Well, the truth is, I would rather see Calvin than Peking, Shanghai, Japan, and forty other places. I cannot even have the satisfaction of a good cry, for Calvin would hear of it, and how worried he would be to hear of tears that he could not wipe away!"

她曾在烟台养病期间说道："每逢星期三登州的信使送信来的时候，我总要照例犯上一阵思乡病，尽管我这里条件优渥，可我却是一周比一周更加瘦弱。唉，说实话，比起北京、上海、日本，还有其他四十个地方，我倒是更想见到柯文。我甚至连痛痛快快哭一场都不能——因为柯文若是听说了，不知该多么担心，听说我流了泪，他却没法替我擦去！"

At length Dr. and Mrs. Wherry, who had just returned from furlough, came to take charge of the school, which rendered it possible for them to take their vacation. Mrs. Mateer had finally been prevailed upon to go some months in advance of her husband, leaving him to put through the press in Shanghai some important literary work. While their outward passage across the Atlantic and Indian Oceans and China Sea required about six months, their return across the Pacific Ocean occupied but one month. When crossing the continent a fellow passenger became acquainted with Mrs. Mateer and found after a day or two that she had been in China. Being a man of the world, a disbeliever in missions, and withal hating the Chinese, he freely expressed his opinion that no Chinaman could become a real Christian. She did not argue on general principles, but told the following story: " One of our converts of some years' standing, a middle-aged farmer, called to see me and tell of his welfare. After some conversation and exhortation, I asked him what evidence he had that he was a Christian. He spoke of his faith in Christ, etc., but I asked him if he had any real, concrete proof. After a moment's reflection, he said: ' I believe I have a little proof. When I used to clear the stones off my fields, because it was most convenient, I just tossed them over into my neighbor's fields, but I don't do that now. I carry and throw them into a gully or some other place, where they will not harm anybody.'" Her listener looked thoughtful for a moment and then said, " There are a good many Christians in America who I fear would find it difficult to give as good an account of the effect of their religion." He said no more against the Chinese or against religion.

终于，刚休假归来的胡维珍博士夫妇（Dr. and Mrs. Wherry）前来接管学校，这才使他们夫妇得以安心休假。狄考文夫人最终被说服，提前丈夫几个月先行动身，留下丈夫在上海料理一些重要的著述付印事宜。他们出发时横渡大西洋、印度洋和中国海，历时约六个月，而归程横渡太平洋，却只花了一个月。在横穿美国大陆的途中，一位旅伴与狄考文夫人攀谈起来，一两天后才得知她曾在中国生活。这人是个见多识广、却不信宣教事业、甚至厌恶中国人的人，他毫不掩饰地表示，他认为中国人根本不可能成为真正的基督徒。她没有从大道理上与他争辩，只是讲了这样一个故事："我们有一位信主已有些年头的信徒，是个中年农夫，有一次来看我，告诉我他近况如何。交谈、劝勉了一番之后，我问他，有什么证据能证明自己是基督徒。他谈起自己对基督的信心之类的话，但我又问他，有没有什么实实在在、看得见的凭据。他略一沉吟，说：'我想我倒是有那么一点小小的凭据。以前我清理地里的石头时，图方便，总是随手扔到邻居的地里去，可如今我不这样做了。我把石头搬到沟里，或是别的什么不会伤到人的地方去扔。'"她的听众若有所思地沉默了片刻，然后说道："我恐怕，美国也有不少基督徒，未必能像这样，拿出这么好的凭据，证明自己的信仰确实产生了果效。"此后，他再没说过什么诋毁中国人或诋毁宗教的话了。

After leaving Omaha, a brisk, young commercial traveller got into conversation with her. He found her so well informed on general topics that he finally asked her for what house she was travelling. She told him that she was travelling for the Lord and had been out on a fifteen years' tour in China.

离开奥马哈之后，一位干练的年轻推销员与她攀谈起来。他发觉她见多识广，对各种话题都十分了解，最后忍不住问她，是替哪家商号跑生意的。她告诉他，自己是替主跑生意的，这一趟"出差"，已经在中国跑了十五年了。

She went directly to her old home at Delaware, Ohio. Her dear old uncle, John Cunningham, and his wife, with whom she as a girl had made her home, were still there to welcome her, and her sister, her cousins and many friends of her girlhood were overjoyed to see her once more. After visiting her own relatives and those of her husband, although still far from well, she spent much time going from place to place, making missionary addresses to societies and bands, arranging three or four addresses a week. In her speaking she was clear in all her statements, no embellishments, no coloring for effect. Yet she looked on the bright side and was intensely in earnest, striving to set forth the needs of the Chinese, especially their spiritual need, and impressing all who heard her with the longing in her own soul for their salvation. Her captivating smile, her sparkling eyes and animated face could not be forgotten, nor the interest with which her quiet humor, her Scriptural allusions, her vigor and charm, invested the telling of her story; but that which remained longest with those who heard her was her simplicity, her single-mindedness and the Christlikeness of her spirit. Her speaking was so effective that she had more invitations than she could possibly accept. Her influence was felt markedly in the homes where she was entertained; but it is to be feared that the visiting and much conversation, probably late into the night, were a great strain on her weak head and back. She was the means of influencing various young people to consecrate their life to mission work.

她径直回到了自己在俄亥俄州特拉华的故居。她亲爱的老舅舅约翰·坎宁安（John Cunningham）和舅母——她少女时代便是在他们家中长大的——仍然健在，热情地迎接她的归来；她的妹妹、表亲以及许多少女时代的旧友，也都为能再次见到她而欣喜若狂。在探望完自己和丈夫双方的亲属之后，尽管身体依然虚弱未愈，她仍花费大量时间四处奔走，向各团体和团契发表宣教演说，一周排下三四场演讲。她讲话时条理清晰、字字属实，从不加以粉饰渲染、刻意煽情。然而，她始终着眼于光明的一面，态度极为恳切，竭力向听众陈明中国人的种种需要——尤其是他们属灵上的需要，也让所有听过她演讲的人，都深深感受到她灵魂深处对他们得救的那份渴望。她那迷人的微笑、闪亮的眼眸和生动的神情，令人难以忘怀；她那沉静的幽默、援引经文的巧思、活力与魅力，也为她的讲述增色不少，令听者兴致盎然；但真正令听众久久难忘的，是她的单纯、她的一心一意，以及她那基督般的灵性。她的演说效果极佳，以致邀约多得她根本应付不过来。她的影响力，在接待她的那些家庭中尤为显著；但恐怕那些拜访和交谈——往往持续到深夜——也令她本就虚弱的头部和背部承受了极大的负荷。正是她，促使不少年轻人立志将一生奉献给宣教事业。

After an exhausting winter, which Mrs. Mateer had hoped might be one of rest and recuperation, she found a refuge at Clifton Springs, that quiet home for worn missionaries. She speaks of the many social demands of loving friends and adds: " In three months of this supposed resting time, I have written over one hundred letters and more than as many postals." She continues: " This week I have been reading over old letters. They showed that we worried much uselessly, and worked unwisely, and that we often ' tithed mint, anise, and cummin' in our zeal, and ' neglected weightier things' in our ignorance; but they show a great deal of fresh enthusiasm which I wish we could have kept."

经过一个原本盼望能得着安歇和调养、却反倒令人心力交瘁的冬天之后，狄考文夫人终于在克利夫顿泉（Clifton Springs）——那处专为疲惫宣教士预备的静养之所——寻得了片刻的庇护。她提到了亲友们种种社交上的殷切要求，又补充道："在这三个月本该用来休养的日子里，我竟写了一百多封信，还有为数相当的明信片。"她接着写道："这一周，我一直在翻看过去的旧信。这些信让我看清，我们曾多么无谓地忧虑、多么不智地操劳，也曾常常出于一腔热忱，'什一奉献了薄荷、茴香、芹菜'，却因无知而'忽略了律法上更重的事'；但这些信里，也透着一股焕然的热忱，我倒真希望我们能一直保有这份热忱。"

It is a matter of great regret that no record was kept of speeches made while at home, but her thought upon the relation of the missionary to the home church is set forth in the extract given below:

令人十分遗憾的是，她在国内所做的这些演讲，并未留下任何记录；但她对宣教士与本国教会之间关系的思考，可见于下文所摘录的这段文字：

" I have received a letter containing these sentences: ' I wish our hearts could be stirred up to some due sense of the demands of the heathen upon us, as you who are surrounded by them see and feel it. Please write to us something to rouse our waning zeal.' We frequently receive similar requests from many different places, and occasionally strong appeals from our secretaries, to help them supply the demand for something to stimulate the interest of the Church. The touching and stirring account of the conversion of an African boy, as given in the Foreign Missionary a few years ago, by one connected with the Zulu Mission, was pointed out to us as an example of what good could be done by well written accounts of incidents we meet with in our work.

"我收到过一封信，里面有这样的话：'但愿我们的心也能被触动，多少感受到你们身处异教徒当中所目睹、所感受到的那份迫切需要。请写点什么来激发我们日渐消退的热忱吧。'我们常常从许多不同的地方收到类似的请求，偶尔还会收到本会秘书们措辞恳切的呼吁，请我们协助满足教会对激发其兴趣之内容的需求。几年前《海外宣教》刊物上，一位与祖鲁差会有关联的人，写下了一则非洲男孩归信的感人故事，这被当作范例，指出若能把我们工作中遇到的种种事迹好好记述下来，将会产生多大的益处。

" Now, there is not one of us who would not be thankful and delighted, and inexpressibly gratified, and perhaps He who knows our hearts best sees that in order to be truthful we must add, mightily elated, to send a touching story by every mail. But whatever may be the reason, there are very few such stories to write. We missionaries are the happiest people in the world, but not chiefly, nor frequently, because of the zeal enkindled by great and startling successes. Missionary work is much like teaching and preaching at home. Some souls are growing in grace, some are coming into the church, and some have gone home to glory. Good is being done all the time, yet at times we sigh more than we rejoice. When the ten lepers were cleansed, one turned back to give thanks, but Christ grieved for the nine. We almost forget the tens that are saved among the tens of thousands who are going down to death. Moreover, we find those who, like the Galatians, ' run well for a time,' and then our rejoicing on their account is turned into mourning. We soon learn to ' rejoice with trembling,' and fear to tell our joy, lest it turn into disappointment. Sometimes we do like David, ' number the people'; and like David, we have to mourn our folly.

"如今，我们当中没有一个人不愿意、不欣喜、不由衷地感激——或许，最了解我们内心的那一位，若要我们说真话，还得加上一句：也不无几分沾沾自喜——若能每次邮件都寄去一则感人的故事的话。但不管是什么原因，能写的这类故事实在寥寥无几。我们这些宣教士，是世上最快乐的人，但这快乐，主要并非、也并不常常，来自那些惊人成功所点燃的热忱。宣教的工作，其实很像国内的教书和讲道。有些灵魂在恩典中渐渐成长，有些人正加入教会，也有些人已经归回天家、得享荣耀。良善的工作，无时无刻不在进行，可我们有时叹息，反倒多过欣喜。十个麻风病人得了洁净，只有一个回头感恩，基督却为那另外九个人忧伤。在成千上万走向死亡的人当中，我们几乎忘记了那少数几十个得救的人。此外，我们还会遇见一些人，像加拉太人一样，'一时奔跑得很好'，可后来，我们为他们所存的喜悦，转眼便化作了哀恸。我们很快便学会了'战兢地快乐'，也害怕轻易道出心中的喜悦，唯恐它转眼变成失望。有时，我们也会像大卫那样，去'数点百姓'；而像大卫一样，我们也不得不为自己的愚妄而哀哭。

" This work taxes not only our minds and our bodies, but our hearts, and that all the time. It is hard for the stream to rise above the fountain. It is hard for the extremities to be warmer than the heart. It is hard for the picket guards to be more enthusiastic than the army. You at home are the fountain; we are the stream. You are the heart; we are the extremities. You are the army; we are the pickets. We are almost cut off from the Christian and civilized world. Our friends become absorbed in their interests, and cares, and new friendships, and one by one forget us, till the mail almost ceases to bring us any but mother's letters and business letters. By and by, mother closes her dim eyes, and folds her trembling hands in her long sleep, while we are far away; and so almost the last link is broken that binds us to a Christian land.

"这份工作，不仅耗损我们的心智和体力，更是无时无刻不在耗损我们的心。溪流很难高过它的源头。四肢很难比心脏更温暖。前哨的哨兵，也很难比大军本身更满怀热忱。你们在本国，就是那源头；我们，是那溪流。你们，是心脏；我们，是四肢。你们，是大军；我们，是哨兵。我们几乎与基督教文明世界完全隔绝。我们的朋友们，渐渐沉浸于自己的种种事务、忧虑和新的交情之中，一个接一个地把我们淡忘，直到寄来的邮件，几乎只剩下母亲的家书和公务信函。渐渐地，母亲也阖上了昏花的双眼，把颤抖的双手叠放，长眠而去，而我们却远在天涯；那几乎是我们与基督教故土之间，最后一根维系的纽带，也就此断绝了。

" Appeals come to us for information. The Church has a right to know what we are doing and what are the results. We are watchmen as well as workmen. We answer as fully as time will allow, but there comes no response. A Sunday-school or society offers to aid in the support of a particular station, or a congregation offers to support a missionary, and all wish frequent and regular letters to keep up the interest. We write our regular letter, they send their regular contribution; but there comes with it no word of sympathy or cheer. All is silent as the grave. Does our zeal never wane? Does your interest never flag? Do our hearts never faint? Are we in no danger of growing weary in well doing? Who writes letters to rouse our waning zeal? Who cheers our drooping spirits? In Christian lands, full of Bibles and churches and Sunday-schools, with thousands of praying men and women, we sing: ' Is this vile world a friend to grace, To help us on to God?'

"呼吁纷纷向我们涌来，索求各种信息。教会有权知道我们在做什么、又有什么成果。我们既是工人，也是守望者。我们尽量在时间允许的范围内，如实作答，可回音却往往杳无踪影。某个主日学班或某个团体，愿意资助某个具体的差会站，或某个会众，愿意供养某位宣教士，他们都希望能收到频繁而规律的书信，好维系这份热忱。我们按规矩写去信函，他们也按规矩寄来捐款；可这捐款之中，却没有半句关切或鼓励的话语。一切都如坟墓般沉寂。难道我们的热忱就永不消退吗？难道你们的关注就永不松懈吗？难道我们的心就永不疲乏吗？难道我们就丝毫没有行善而生厌倦的危险吗？谁来写信，激发我们那日渐消退的热忱？谁来鼓舞我们那渐渐低落的心志？在满是圣经、教堂和主日学、又有成千上万祷告的男男女女的基督教国度里，你们尚且唱着：'这污秽的世界，岂是恩典的良友，能助我们迈向神？'

" What, then, must the heathen world be? You must not think it is only the Chinese Christians who are affected by the surrounding heathenism. It tries the grace of the best and strongest of us. How can a few rushlights light up this dense darkness? Much less can they shine out to light and stimulate those who live in the brightness of the gospel day. Is not the wonder rather that they are not quenched in the darkness?

"那么，异教徒的世界，又该是何等光景呢？切莫以为，只有中国的基督徒才会受到周遭异教氛围的侵蚀。这也在考验我们当中最优秀、最坚强之人的恩典。区区几支小小的灯芯烛火，又怎能照亮这浓重的黑暗？更遑论要发出光来，照亮、激励那些原本就生活在福音光明之中的人们了。这难道不是更令人惊叹的事吗——它们竟没有在这黑暗中被扑灭？

" My sisters, the conversion of the world is not a job to be finished in a year or two, by a spurt now and then, when some extra excitement stirs up our enthusiasm. It is a stupendous work that will require the concentrated and consecrated energies of all who love the Lord Jesus, of every name and nation, and for many long years to come. The only way to accomplish it is for the whole Church to go at the work in obedience to our Lord's command, with a just estimate of its immensity and with a strong determination on the part of each member to work on until he falls at his post. There is no discharge in this warfare, and it will not be ended in our day.

"姊妹们，使世界归信，绝非一年两载便能完工的差事，也绝非靠着偶尔一时兴起的热忱、断断续续地拼一阵子便能成就的。这是一项浩大无比的工程，需要一切爱主耶稣之人——不分名号、不分国籍——投入全部专一而奉献的心力，还需要许多、许多年月才能完成。要成就这项工程，唯一的办法，便是整个教会都遵从我们主的命令，投身其中，正确估量其浩瀚，每一位成员都下定坚强的决心，坚持工作，直至倒在自己的岗位上。这是一场没有退役的争战，也绝不会在我们这一代人手中结束。

" A geography will be the best help to a just estimate of its immensity; and the Bible the only help to a just estimate of its difficulty and importance, because that alone reveals to us the depravity of the human heart and the value of human souls. When our zeal wanes, let us renew it in our closets. When our courage fails, let us strengthen ourselves in the Lord our God. When our labors seem fruitless, let us go to the sure promises. When we are tempted to despair by reason of the ingratitude, opposition, and hatred of those to whom we are giving our money, our labor, and our lives, let us look unto Jesus, our great example, and endure, as He did, unto the end, ' for the joy that is set before us.' We will try to tell you all the stirring facts the Lord gives us, and give you all the information our strength and time will allow, but do not depend upon us for a motive, or the measure of your zeal and consecration. No motive but a loving obedience to Christ will sustain either you or us to the end of this mighty work. May the whole Church in all lands be soon brought up to the requisite degree of faith and consecration."

"一本地理书，最能帮助我们正确估量这项工程的浩瀚；而唯有圣经，才能帮助我们正确估量它的艰巨与重大，因为唯有圣经，才向我们揭示了人心的败坏和人灵魂的价值。当我们的热忱消退时，让我们到密室中重新点燃它。当我们的勇气衰败时，让我们在主我们的神里刚强壮胆。当我们的劳苦似乎毫无果效时，让我们转向那确实的应许。当我们因那些接受我们金钱、劳力和生命之人的忘恩负义、抵挡和憎恨，而陷入绝望的试探时，让我们仰望耶稣——我们伟大的榜样，效法祂那样，'因那摆在前面的喜乐'，忍耐到底。我们会尽力把主所赐下的一切激励人心的事实，都告诉你们，也会在我们的精力和时间所允许的范围内，把一切信息都提供给你们；但请不要仅仅依赖我们，来作为你们热忱和奉献的动力或衡量标准。除了出于爱而顺服基督之外，没有任何别的动机，能支撑你们或我们，坚持到这项伟大工程的终点。愿普天下各地的教会，都能迅速达到所必需的那种信心与奉献的程度。"

The following is a letter written to a young lady whom Mrs. Mateer had known eight years before, inviting her to join the Tengchow circle:

以下是她写给一位年轻女子的一封信，这位女子是她八年前便已相识的旧交，此信是邀请她加入登州的宣教团队：

" I am writing because I have something special to say to you — to ask of you. But first promise not to decide yea or nay until you have looked at the question seriously, spread it out before Jesus, and let Him decide it for you. He will do it if you are really and heartily willing.

"我提笔写信，是因为有一件特别的事，要对你说——要向你恳求。但请先答应我，在你郑重考虑这个问题、把它摆在耶稣面前、任由祂为你决定之前，不要轻易说是或说否。只要你是真心诚意地愿意，祂必会为你做这个决定。

" My proposition is, that you give yourself to the foreign missionary work. I know it is a very serious undertaking for a young woman who has never been separated from her parents even for a few months, and especially one who has gone abroad but little, to start on a journey half-way around the globe to spend her life with — for the most part — strangers.

"我要向你提出的，是希望你能够献身于海外宣教事业。我知道，对于一个从未与父母分离哪怕几个月的年轻女子，尤其是一个甚少出国的人来说，要启程踏上这样一趟绕半个地球的旅程，去与——多半是——陌生人共度余生，实在是一件极其重大的抉择。

" But that is not the point from which to look at this question. It is this: What has Jesus done for you, what is He doing day by day, and what do you yet hope from His rich grace? In view of all this, what are you willing to do for Him? What sacrifice can you make for Him to be at all compared with the sacrifice He has made for you? The great command, ' Go ye into all the world and preach the Gospel to every creature,' is to every child of God, even to the youngest, the weakest, the most ignorant. The question each must decide is, ' How am I called to help on this great work?' It may lie in going to teach the heathen; it may be by giving up a very dear friend to go; it may be chiefly by a life of prayer and cheerful devotion to the humblest duties. I think the first is perhaps your duty, and my object in writing is to suggest the subject to you, and if possible aid you in making a decision.

"但这并不是看待这个问题应有的出发点。真正的出发点应当是：耶稣为你做了什么，祂日复一日又在为你做什么，你还盼望从祂丰盛的恩典中得着什么？想到这一切，你又愿意为祂做些什么呢？你所能做出的任何牺牲，与祂为你所做的牺牲相比，又算得了什么呢？那伟大的命令——'你们往普天下去，传福音给万民听'——是对每一个神的儿女说的，甚至是对最年幼、最软弱、最无知的人说的。每个人都必须自己去回答这个问题：'我该如何蒙召，来助成这项伟大的工程？'这也许在于亲身前去教导异教徒；也许在于甘愿放弃一位至亲的挚友，让他前去；也许主要在于过一种祷告的生活，甘心乐意地献身于最卑微的职分。我想，前者或许正是你的本分所在，我写这封信的目的，正是要向你提出这个问题，并尽可能地帮助你作出决定。

" One reason I think you are called to the missionary work is your fondness for teaching, and the success which has attended your efforts. The whole missionary work, as far as the human agent is concerned, is teaching; and I have known no instance of a successful teacher failing in this work. This, then, is the great qualification God has given you. If your gift is fully consecrated to Jesus, you are fit to be a missionary. If it is not consecrated, you will not be, in the highest sense, a successful teacher at home.

"我之所以认为你蒙召从事宣教工作，理由之一，便是你对教学的热爱，以及你在这方面所取得的成就。就人力所能及的层面而言，整个宣教事业，说到底就是教学；而我从未见过一位成功的教师，在这项工作上会失败的。这，便是神赐给你的一项极大的资质。若你的这份恩赐，全然奉献给耶稣，你便适合成为一名宣教士。若不然，即便你留在国内，从最高的意义上说，也算不得一位真正成功的教师。

" Many people have vague ideas as to what missionary work is. Missionaries and their work are to them surrounded by a kind of religious romance which seems very attractive in the dim distance, but they have very little living interest in the true picture. Nothing can be more real and practical than the missionary work — I might add, more monotonous, but it is just the monotony of school life, teaching the same things over and over to one class after another, year after year, — the same lessons, but the class is ever varying, and each individual learner brings new interest and new incentives to exertion.

"许多人对宣教工作究竟是什么，只有一个模糊的概念。在他们看来，宣教士和他们的工作，似乎笼罩着一层宗教式的浪漫色彩，远远望去令人心生向往，但对于其中的真实图景，却鲜有真切的兴趣。世上再没有比宣教工作更真实、更务实的事了——我不妨再补一句，也没有比它更单调乏味的了，但那不过是学校生活本身的那种单调罢了——年复一年，把同样的内容，一遍又一遍地教给一批又一批的学生——课程虽是一样的，学生却总在变化，每一个学生个体，都会带来新的兴致和新的努力动力。

" As in teaching at home, so it is here; sometimes one is encouraged to enthusiasm, sometimes ready to faint. Here, as there, faith and perseverance will bring you through. The missionary life is one of toil and many trials, but it is by no means all shadow; indeed, it is far more nearly all sunshine.

"正如在国内教书一样，这里的情形也是这样——有时你会因受到鼓舞而满怀热忱，有时又几乎要心灰意冷。这里也和那里一样，唯有信心与坚忍，才能带你渡过难关。宣教士的生活，是辛劳与重重试炼交织而成的，但绝非尽是阴霾；事实上，它几乎处处是阳光。

" You will find the work in an important sense its own reward. Though you will see but little of society, it will be made up of men and women of education and refinement, many of whom would adorn any society. You can have all the comforts of a plain family at home. You will find in the customs of these Oriental people many striking comments on the Bible, and much of interest. Learning a new language will improve your mind.

"你会发现，从一个重要的意义上说，这项工作本身便是它自己的报偿。虽然你所能接触的社交圈子不大，但那圈子里的人，无不是受过良好教育、气质高雅之人，许多人放在任何社交场合，都不失为翘楚。你在这里，也能享有寻常人家一切朴素的舒适。你还会发现，这些东方民族的种种习俗，与圣经的教导之间，有着许多引人入胜的呼应和意趣。学习一门新的语言，也会让你的心智更加开阔。

" There will, of course, be much opposition to your devoting yourself to this work. You will often hear, ' Why go away there? Are there not heathen enough at home?' Of course there are. Were there not many unbelieving Jews in Judea when Christ bade His disciples go into all the world? Were there not heathen enough in and around Antioch, when Paul and Silas were sent out thence as missionaries? Were there no more heathen in Asia, when Paul was called into Macedonia? What claims have American heathen on you that Chinese or Hindu heathen have not?

"当然，你若要献身于这项工作，必定会遭遇诸多的反对。你常常会听到有人说：'何必跑到那么远的地方去？难道本国的异教徒还不够多吗？'的确，本国的异教徒确实不少。可当初基督吩咐门徒往普天下去的时候，犹太地岂不也有许多不信的犹太人吗？保罗和西拉从安提阿被差遣出去作宣教士的时候，安提阿及其周边，岂不也有足够多的异教徒吗？保罗蒙召前往马其顿的时候，亚细亚岂不也还有更多的异教徒吗？美国的异教徒对你有什么特殊的权利要求，是中国或印度的异教徒所没有的呢？

" In the United States there must be at least one minister for every two or three thousand persons, besides all the Sabbath-school teachers and other Christian men and women who are working and praying for the unconverted. In China, counting ministers, and male and female teachers, native and foreign, there is scarcely one religious teacher of any grade for a million.

"在美国，大概每两三千人，便至少配有一位牧师，此外还有无数的主日学教师和其他基督徒男女，都在为未归信的人辛勤劳作、恳切祷告。而在中国，算上牧师，以及本地和外国的男女教师，每百万人中，几乎找不出一位任何等级的宗教教师来。

" Women in Christian lands can never know what they owe the Bible until they see the deep, utter degradation, — mental, moral and physical — of heathen women. And the wretched heathen women are our sisters, as good by nature as we, and as deserving of God's favor. If, then, we have been singled out to receive such blessings, shall we deny to them the lamp of life? ' If the watchman failed to warn the city, the guilt was his.' (See Ezekiel, thirty-third chapter.)

"基督教国度的妇女，若不曾亲眼目睹异教妇女那种深重、彻底的堕落——心智上的、道德上的、肉体上的堕落——便永远无从体会自己究竟从圣经中蒙受了多大的恩惠。而那些可怜的异教妇女，正是我们的姊妹，本性与我们并无二致，同样配得神的恩宠。既然我们蒙拣选、领受了这样的福分，我们又怎能拒绝把这生命之光，也分给她们呢？'倘若守望的人不发出警号提醒众人，罪责就在他身上。'（参以西结书第三十三章）

" I could write a volume on the need of teachers here, especially earnest, self-denying women. How shall I compress it into the space of one letter! I can only refer you to the first chapter of Romans for a description of all heathen people, and to the tenth chapter as to the way they are to be brought out of it."

"关于此地对教师的需求——尤其是对那些恳切、克己的妇女的需求——我大可写上厚厚一卷。可我又怎能把这一切压缩进一封信里呢！我只能请你去读罗马书第一章，那里描述了一切异教徒的光景；再读第十章，那里讲明了他们该如何从中被引领出来。"

After advising the young lady to seek all light possible on the subject, she says: " But I advise you not to consult too much with human friends, especially with those who have never given the subject a serious thought. After eight years of trial, and an average share of work and weariness, and all the ordinary ills of this kind of life, I have never for one moment regretted entering upon it. The only regret I have ever felt was that I did not begin earlier, that I might have had more years to devote to it. My sister is of the same mind.

在劝勉这位年轻女子尽力寻求各方面的亮光之后，她又写道："但我劝你，不要过多地去征询俗世朋友的意见，尤其是那些从未认真思考过这个问题的人。经过八年的历练，尝过了寻常份内的辛劳与疲惫，也历经了这种生活中种种寻常的病痛，我却从未有一刻后悔踏上这条路。我唯一感到遗憾的，是自己没有更早开始，好能有更多的年月投入其中。我妹妹也有同样的心思。

" Separation from your family and friends will be a severe trial, but He who calls for the sacrifice will sustain you and them. Should He call you away from earth, could any human love stay you? Should He call you to the uttermost parts of the earth to serve Him, may any mortal oppose His will?

"与家人朋友分离，固然是一场严酷的试炼，但那呼召你作出这份牺牲的主，必会扶持你，也必扶持他们。倘若祂要召你离开这个世界，可有任何人间的爱能够留住你？倘若祂要差你前往地极去事奉祂，又岂是任何凡人可以违逆祂的旨意的呢？

" This question must be decided by yourself in your closet. Do not say ' No' unless you can give a reason which you are sure will stand at the bar of God; nor ' Yes' unless you can also give a reason. Dr. Plummer says, ' It is a sin to run when we are not called, as much as not to run when we are called.'"

"这个问题，必须由你自己在密室中决定。不要轻易说'不'，除非你有把握，这个理由能在神的审判台前站立得住；也不要轻易说'是'，除非你同样能给出理由。普卢默博士（Dr. Plummer）说过：'在不蒙召的时候擅自奔跑，与在蒙召的时候却不肯奔跑，二者同样都是罪。'"

With regard to the position of a missionary's wife, this is Mrs. Mateer's feeling: " Though a missionary's wife may from whatever cause be unable to do much direct missionary work herself, yet if she have the right spirit, her deep sympathetic interest will be a great help to her husband; whereas one lacking this spirit will be a decided handicap, if indeed she do not cause his complete and pathetic failure."

关于宣教士妻子所处的地位，狄考文夫人是这样看的："宣教士的妻子，无论出于何种缘故，纵使不能亲身从事多少直接的宣教工作，但只要她怀有正确的心志，她那份深切而富有同情的关注，便会成为丈夫极大的助力；反之，若她缺乏这样的心志，便会成为丈夫明显的拖累，甚至可能导致他彻头彻尾、令人痛心的失败。"

Concerning single women she writes: " The inconsiderate haste with which some women have made up their minds to come to China and their failure to appreciate the work to be done, and the trials to be borne, have astonished me. This has made me feel guilty, that when I had opportunity to talk with young people at home I did not enlarge and insist much on the solemn responsibility of undertaking this work, presenting it as a warfare in which there can be few furloughs, and no honorable discharge but death. I do not forget the longing that every true woman has for a home, for I have been a homeless orphan; nor would I underestimate the happiness of married life — no happy wife can. I know, too, that it is no light thing for a woman to leave friends and home and all the blessings of a Christian land, and go out alone, to bear the toils and cares, the responsibilities and disappointments, the weariness and loneliness of a missionary life in China. But God will give grace even for this to every one whom He calls to it. They who endure to the end will find deep and abiding happiness in this work."

关于独身女子，她写道："有些妇女仓促草率地便下定决心要来中国，却对将要面对的工作和须承受的种种试炼缺乏应有的认识，这实在令我震惊。这也让我心生愧疚——每逢在国内有机会与年轻人交谈，我却未能充分强调、坚持申明担负这项工作那份严肃的责任，未能把它讲明为一场少有休假、除死亡之外别无光荣退役之日的争战。我并未忘记，每个真正的女子内心，都渴望有一个家——因为我自己便曾是一个无家可归的孤儿；我也绝不会低估婚姻生活的幸福——没有哪个幸福的妻子会小看这一点。我同样深知，一个女子要离开亲友和家园，离开基督教国度的种种福分，独自出行，去承受在中国宣教生活中的辛劳与忧虑、责任与失望、疲惫与孤独，这绝非一件轻而易举的小事。但神必赐下恩典，让每一位祂所呼召的人，都能承受得起，即便是这些。凡坚持到底的人，终将在这项工作中，寻得深沉而恒久的幸福。"

" One woman, after twelve years of single-handed toil in this place, said on her death-bed: ' If Christians at home only knew the blessedness of this work, there would be nobody willing to stay at home.' A whole-hearted devotion to it will secure this happiness; nothing else will.

"有一位妇女，在此地独自辛劳了十二年之后，临终时说道：'倘若本国的基督徒也能明白这份工作的福祉，恐怕就没有人愿意留在家里了。'唯有全心全意的委身，才能获得这份幸福；除此之外，别无他法。

" A young woman who offers herself for this work should be very sure that her temperament and sense of duty will, through grace, sustain her to the end. She should know her own mind and heart and body before she decides to be a single missionary. She should come with the simple and honest purpose of devoting her whole life to the work, and she should throw all her energies into it, expecting to bear her share of the burdens, and to find her chief happiness in the work."

"一个愿意献身于这项工作的年轻女子，务必要确信，凭借神的恩典，自己的性情和责任心，能够支撑她坚持到底。她应当在决心成为一名独身宣教士之前，先认清自己的心思、心志和体魄。她应当抱着单纯而真诚的心愿而来，将自己的一生完全奉献给这项工作，全力以赴地投入其中，预备好承担自己应尽的那份重担，并从这工作本身，寻得她最大的幸福。"

第十三章 · Chapter 13 日落钟声 Sunset and Evening Bells

THIS life of gracious beauty and blessed result drew painfully to its earthly ending. It was on February 18, 1898, after fourteen weeks of continuous and intense suffering that the grateful release came. During the previous winter Mrs. Mateer had been compelled to undergo a surgical operation. From this she gradually recovered. But during the last few years there had been many periods of complete prostration, followed by shorter and shorter times of activity, until in November, 1897, she lay down never to rise again. The " silver cord " had undergone such a long, continuous strain that nothing medical skill could suggest could save it from being " loosened." The rheumatic pains subsided after the eighth week; but the nervous exhaustion increased more and more. She was weary unto death, and restless in the extreme. Sleep brought neither rest nor refreshment, and constant changing of position no relief. Her appetite failed, and the difficulty of taking nourishment increased. Thus she continued suffering, and waiting, and longing for the end.

这一生充满优美恩典、结出蒙福果实的生命，终究痛苦地走向了她在世的尽头。1898年2月18日，在经历了整整十四周持续而剧烈的病痛之后，那令人感恩的解脱终于降临。前一年冬天，狄考文夫人曾被迫接受了一次外科手术。术后她逐渐康复。但在最后的几年间，她屡屡陷入完全虚脱的状态，而恢复活动的时日却一次比一次短暂，直到1897年11月，她终于卧床不起，再未能起身。那"银链"历经了如此漫长、持续的拉扯，以致任何医术都已无力阻止它被"折断"。风湿的疼痛在第八周之后逐渐消退，但神经的衰竭却愈发加重。她已是疲惫至死，极度地辗转不安。睡眠既不能带来休息，也不能带来恢复；不断变换姿势，也无法减轻分毫痛楚。她的食欲渐渐衰退，进食也愈发困难。她就这样，一直忍受着病痛，等候着，盼望着终点的到来。

Throughout all these weeks of physical agony students were home on vacation, the large church was crowded with sorrowing Chinese, both Christian and heathen, especially women, who followed to the grave, knowing that the friend who loved them best was gone. One of their number voiced with trembling emphasis the common feeling when he said: " Alas, we have all lost the support upon which we leaned." A young college teacher in his address did not hesitate to say that Mrs. Mateer had done what no other woman had done or could do. His praise was excessive, perhaps, but it was sincere, and within the sphere of his knowledge it was true. He said that a great part of the success of the college was due to her, and that without her it could not have succeeded as it had.

在这几周肉体饱受煎熬的日子里，正逢学生放假在家，那座大教堂里挤满了满心哀伤的中国人，无论是基督徒还是异教徒，尤以妇女居多，她们一路相送，直至墓前，深知这位最疼爱她们的朋友已然离去。其中一人以颤抖而恳切的语气，道出了众人共同的心声："唉，我们所有人，都失去了曾经倚靠的那根支柱。"一位年轻的学院教师在悼词中，毫不迟疑地说，狄考文夫人所做的，是任何别的女子都未曾做到、也无法做到的事。他的赞誉或许有些过誉，但那份真挚却是发自肺腑，而且就他所知的范围而言，句句属实。他说，学院之所以能取得如此成就，很大程度上要归功于她，若没有她，学院断不可能取得这般成绩。

One said: " When a missionary dies, many Chinese Christians look up through their tears with rejoicing, and thank God that a true friend now awaits them in heaven. When I think of Mrs. Mateer I seem to see her standing at the portals of the shining gates, welcoming with her rare, sweet smile the poor, hesitating, timid soul of a Chinese Christian."

有人说："每逢有宣教士去世，许多中国基督徒总会含泪仰望，心中却又满怀喜悦，感谢神如今在天上有一位挚友在等候着他们。每当我想起狄考文夫人，我仿佛看见她正站在那金光闪耀的天门口，带着她那份难得的、甜美的微笑，迎接每一个胆怯、迟疑的中国基督徒灵魂。"

Upon the death of Dr. Mateer, September 28, 1908, her remains were removed to Chefoo, where she lies beside her husband in the beautiful cemetery on the hill.

1908年9月28日，狄考文博士去世后，她的遗骸被迁至烟台，如今她就安葬在丈夫身旁，在那座山丘上美丽的墓园之中。

Mrs. Mateer, though dead, still lives in the transformed lives of the many young men to whom her touch imparted the virtue of Christlike ideals and hopes, in the strength of which they are laboring, suffering and praying for their own people. She lives in the earnest words she spoke by the wayside, in the village and in the home; in the pervasive influence of a noble life that widens and deepens as the years roll on. No pen can trace the fruitage of a life so well lived and a work so well done. Even the analysis of a character of such richness is a difficult undertaking. As one thinks of her, so many lovely qualities flash their light upon one: her sweet temper; her delightful sense of humor, proving often so serviceable a support to her patience and courage; her tact and practical resourcefulness; her gentleness; and along with a firmness and decision that could seem almost stern, the spontaneity, the touch of gaiety, the welling up of fresh interest that kept for her to the end the heart of a child.

狄考文夫人虽然去世了，但她的生命，仍活在她曾亲手触动、赋予基督般理想与盼望之美德的许多年轻人身上，他们正凭借着这份力量，为自己的同胞劳作、受苦、祈祷。她也活在她曾在路旁、在乡村、在家中所说过的那些恳切话语里；活在这一高尚生命所留下的、随岁月流转而愈发广泛深远的持久影响之中。没有任何一支笔，能够描绘出如此善度的一生、如此善成的一番工作所结出的果实。即便只是分析这样一个如此丰富的人格，也已是极为困难的一件事。每当想起她，便有那么多可爱的品质，在人心中一一闪现：她那甜美的性情；她那令人愉悦的幽默感，常常成为她忍耐与勇气极为得力的支撑；她的机敏和务实的应变能力；她的温柔；而与这些相伴的，还有一种近乎严峻的坚定与果断，以及那份自发的、带着几分欢快的天性，那不断涌现的清新兴致——正是这些，让她直到人生的最后，始终保有一颗孩童般的心。

Of her more marked characteristics we venture to say that self-forgetfulness should stand pre-eminent. Even during those long weeks of suffering and weariness unto death, the first thought of Mrs. Mateer was not for herself, but for those who cared for her. Her soft, sweetly spoken " Thank you " for the slightest service rendered, will remain in the hearts of those who were privileged to be with her like a rare fragrance. Equally touching and tender were her words of exhortation to every Chinese friend who came to her bedside. When her mind was wandering she would bring it back with a mighty effort, and give just the right word to the right one. Usually the burden of her message was the sinfulness of sin, and Christ's pardoning love. Many prayers were offered for her, especially by the young men who had been under her instruction. She constantly desired that these prayers might be answered in blessings on the heads of those who offered them.

在她诸多鲜明的品质当中，我们不妨大胆地说，忘我无私应当居于首位。即便是在那漫长的、饱受病痛煎熬、疲惫至死的数周里，狄考文夫人心里首先想到的，从来不是自己，而是那些照料她的人。她那轻柔而甜美地道出的"谢谢你"，哪怕只是为了最微不足道的一点服侍，都会如一缕难得的芬芳，长久留存在有幸陪伴她的人心中。同样感人至深的，是她对每一位前来床边探望的中国朋友所说的劝勉之言。当她神志恍惚之际，她也会竭尽全力，把心思拉回来，对每一个人说出恰如其分的话语。她所要传达的信息，大抵不外乎罪的罪性，以及基督赦罪的大爱。许多人为她祷告，尤其是那些曾受过她教导的年轻人。她总是殷切地盼望，这些祷告最终能化作祝福，临到那些献上祷告之人的头上。

Her husband says: " I started to Weihsien by previous engagement on the nineteenth of August to teach in the theological class. I did not know how badly my wife was feeling at the time. She held back her weakness and nervousness from me, lest I should be hindered in doing the important work I was going to Weihsien to do. All the while she kept writing hopeful letters, thinking she would be better as the weather should grow cool and bracing. But her appetite and her strength continued to fail gradually until she had to go to bed. Her niece took the responsibility of telegraphing me, when I came home as soon as possible." What an eloquent tribute! As the previous pages attest, it was simply in keeping with the principle of self-forgetfulness that had ruled in all her life, and had made her a Christlike power among the Chinese, who are so quick of discernment.

她的丈夫说："8月19日，我按早先的约定，动身前往潍县，去神学班授课。当时我并不知道，我妻子的身体已经糟糕到什么地步。她一直向我隐瞒着自己的虚弱和神经衰弱，唯恐因此耽误了我此去潍县所要完成的这项重要工作。她始终坚持给我写着满怀希望的信，以为等天气转凉、变得清爽一些，她的身体便会好转。但她的食欲和体力却持续渐渐衰退，直到她不得不卧床不起。是她的侄女承担起责任，给我拍了电报，我这才尽快赶回了家。"这是何等有力的一份见证啊！正如前面几章所述，这不过是她一生所秉持的忘我无私之原则的又一次体现，而正是这份原则，使她在那些洞察力敏锐的中国人当中，成为了一股基督般的力量。

A fellow missionary says: " The memory of Mrs. Mateer is very tender, full of all that is helpful and true. Her utterly unselfish life, her Christlike character and her inspiring devotion to His cause and service, endeared her to all."

一位同工的宣教士说："对狄考文夫人的追忆，是那样温柔动人，充满了一切有益而真实的东西。她那毫无私心的一生、她那基督般的品格，以及她对主的事工那份令人感佩的忠诚与奉献，令她赢得了所有人的爱戴。"

An old friend writes: " Our heavenly Father, for a reason we do not understand, which nevertheless we know was lovingly wise, never gave to Mrs. Mateer the joy of motherhood; but He gave her in large measure the heart of motherhood, which spent the rich fullness of its mothering love upon her schoolboys, and indeed upon every one with whom she came in contact. We rarely see a heart so much at leisure from itself, to soothe and sympathize. Freely and in utter self-forgetfulness, she loved, ministered to and watched over the hundreds of Chinese sons and daughters whom God gave to her."

一位故交写道："我们在天上的父，出于我们所不能理解、却深知必是慈爱而智慧的缘由，从未赐给狄考文夫人做母亲的喜乐；但祂却大大地赐给了她一颗母亲的心，这颗心，将其丰盛的母爱，倾注在了她的学生们身上，也倾注在每一个与她相遇之人的身上。我们很少见到有哪一颗心，能像她这样，如此地忘却自我，只顾着去抚慰、去体恤别人。她全然忘我地、自由地去爱、去服侍、去看顾着那成百上千位神所赐给她的中国儿女。"

Humility of spirit must come next — the rare gifts and graces, and the manifold achievements kept by this " clothing of humility " free from the stains of earth. What charm, and what power in it all! It was Mrs. Mateer's habit to speak very humbly of her own attainments in the Christian life and her share in the work accomplished. While in the home-land she once said: " I have been able to do so little — it all comes down to being a ' neighbor' to those Chinese boys and women." The year before her death she wrote: " Lately I have enjoyed a sense of God's nearness, and Jesus has seemed nearer than ever before; yet I do not enjoy prayer as I ought to do. Indeed I am a poor, weak Christian — very, to be filling so responsible a place. I have always been credited above my deserts. That is one of the rewards, perhaps, of being so far away."

其次必得提到的，是她那谦卑的心灵——正是这件"谦卑的外衣"，使她那罕有的恩赐与美德、以及她那多方面的成就，得以不沾染世俗的污秽。这其中，蕴含着何等的魅力，又蕴含着何等的力量啊！狄考文夫人素来习惯于极为谦卑地谈论自己在基督徒生命中的造诣，以及自己在所成就的事工中所占的一份。她在国内时，曾说过："我能做的实在太少了——归根到底，不过是给那些中国的孩子和妇女，做一个'好邻居'罢了。"在她去世前一年，她写道："近来我常常感受到神与我的亲近，耶稣似乎也比以往任何时候都更贴近我；然而，我在祷告上，却始终未能达到自己应有的享受。说实话，我不过是个软弱可怜的基督徒——实在是太软弱了，却要担负如此重大的职责。我素来总是被人高估了。或许，这正是身处这般遥远之地所换来的一种回报吧。"

Little has been said about Mrs. Mateer's prayer-life, partly because of its sacredness, and partly because of her self-effacement and distaste for devotional display. In speaking of consecration she thus expresses herself: " Somehow I believe in the kind that like charity ' vaunteth not itself.' I have not seen that God sends that kind so much through holiness and consecration meetings as through other channels, mostly painful, but sometimes only the common effects of grace on peculiarly adapted characteristics. Perhaps it takes more grace to make some of us decent Christians than to make others eminent saints."

关于狄考文夫人的祷告生活，书中着墨不多，一方面是因为这原本就是极为神圣私密之事，另一方面，也是因为她生性谦抑、不喜张扬灵修表现。谈到"奉献"这一话题时，她曾这样表白："不知怎的，我所信的那种奉献，是像爱一样'不张狂'的那一种。我并未看见神多是通过圣洁会或奉献特会来赐下这样的奉献，反倒更多是通过别的途径——大多是痛苦的途径，但有时也不过是恩典施加在某些特别禀性之人身上所产生的寻常果效罢了。也许，要把我们当中的某些人，造就成一个说得过去的基督徒，所需要的恩典，反倒比把另一些人造就成杰出的圣徒还要更多。"

But, in proportion as she distrusted her own strength, did she rest her faith in the power of prayer. Her sister, of kindred spirit, gathered a company of us young missionaries around her dying bed in order to urge us not to make the fatal mistake of allowing work to crowd out the time necessary for the cultivation of personal piety. Mrs. Mateer says: " What a comfort that so many loved ones pray for us and our work. Many pray for the work, and never think that the workers are poor sinners like themselves, needing constant supplies of grace, and therefore needing the prayers of the Church." Again: " I feel sure the success we have achieved is largely in answer to faithful, fervent prayer at home, and I wonder sometimes whether at the great day it will not appear that the believing, praying people at home have after all done the work, while we have been getting the credit for it."

然而，她越是不信靠自己的力量，便越是把信心安放在祷告的能力之上。与她同气相求的妹妹，也曾在临终的病榻旁，召集我们这些年轻宣教士，恳切嘱咐我们，千万不要犯下让工作挤占了培养个人灵修所需时间这一致命的错误。狄考文夫人说："想到有这么多亲爱的人，在为我们和我们的工作代祷，这是何等大的安慰啊。许多人为这工作祷告，却从未想过，做这工作的人，也和他们自己一样，不过是可怜的罪人，同样需要恩典源源不断的供应，因此也同样需要教会的祷告。"她又说："我深信，我们所取得的成就，很大程度上是本国信实、恳切的祷告所换来的答复，我有时甚至在想，到了那大日子来临之时，最终显明的，恐怕会是本国那些信心坚定、恳切祷告的人，才是真正成就这工作的人，而我们不过是徒然领受了这份功劳罢了。"

Her capacity for devotion and enthusiasm richly deserve mention. She put labor, and strength, and heart into whatever she undertook. With untiring industry and perseverance she wrought, doing an amount of work even when suffering from impaired health that would have been a credit to any one. The influence of all this was, of course, very stimulating. She was a good listener, — all interested devotion to the one with whom she was speaking, so that hearts were unburdened to her as those of children to their mother. Her pupils not only loved and honored her, but they were led to do their very best in every way for her, and for her approval, while life-enthusiasms were kindled at the altar of her consuming loyalty to Christ Jesus. She had a beautiful capacity for joy in simple things, and a nature gifted in finding occasions for happiness and gratitude. Of her rare devotion to her husband and her loyalty to all that engaged him, it is difficult to speak. She often hid herself in his work, and she spent herself lavishly for all that could further it. Speaking once to an intimate friend of how a true wife's heart thrilled as she heard her husband's steps, she nevertheless led the listener's thought up to " Thy Maker is thy Husband." The human love so precious and beautiful but interpreted the supreme devotion of her spirit. She was only happy as " Hid in Him."

她那份投入与热忱的能力，实在值得大书特书。无论承担什么事，她都倾尽劳力、体力和心力去做。她凭着不知疲倦的勤勉与坚忍去做工，即便是在健康受损、饱受病痛之时，她所完成的工作量，也足以让任何人都感到汗颜。这一切所带来的影响，自然也极具感染力。她是个极好的倾听者——无论与谁交谈，她都全神贯注、满怀关切，以致人们向她倾诉衷肠时，就如同孩子向母亲倾诉一般毫无保留。她的学生不仅爱戴、敬重她，还因她而受到激励，在各方面都竭尽全力去做到最好，只为博得她的赞许；他们一生的热忱，也在她那对基督耶稣全然的忠诚祭坛前，得以点燃。她天生便具有一种从简单事物中寻得喜乐的美好能力，也天生善于从中发掘出令人快乐和感恩的时机。至于她对丈夫那份罕有的忠诚，以及她对丈夫所投身的一切事业那份忠贞，实在难以尽述。她常常把自己隐没在丈夫的工作之中，不遗余力地为一切能推进这份工作的事情，倾尽自己的心力。有一次，她对一位知己谈起，一个真正的妻子，听见丈夫的脚步声时，内心是何等的悸动，然而她却借此，把听者的思绪，引向了"造你的是你的丈夫"这句话。那份珍贵而美好的人间爱情，不过是诠释了她灵魂深处那至高无上的忠诚罢了。唯有"隐藏在祂里面"，她才能真正得着喜乐。

She had a broad sympathy and an inexhaustible charity. Dr. Ellinwood, the great Board secretary, said: " I have for years thought of her as a model. Certainly without a superior anywhere, of either sex, in the whole missionary force which I have known in these twenty-six years. So full of broad sympathy and kindliness, so absolutely balanced and level in common sense, so genuine in piety, so peace-loving and salutary in all her relations to other missionaries." Dr. W. A. P. Martin says: " The dear one who has gone I greatly admired for her quiet power, unwearied devotion and inexhaustible charity." She fulfilled Paul's injunction in Philippians ii. 3, where (as we see by the original) he counsels us " to lay our life down like a soft tapestry carpet, in kindly thoughts and gracious sympathies and helpful services, in order that the weary, bruised feet of other people may find ease and comfort on the road." And God only knows how for many of the Chinese " the ways of life are very rough and flinty, and the sharp, jagged edges of circumstance cut the feet most sorely."

她怀有宽广的同情心和取之不尽的爱心。差会理事会的大秘书艾林伍德博士曾说："多年来，我一直把她视为一个典范。在我这二十六年所结识的整个宣教队伍中，无论男女，确实无人能出其右。她怀着这般宽广的同情与仁慈，在常理常识上又是那样地平衡稳重，虔诚又是那样地真挚，在与其他宣教士的一切往来中，都是那样地爱好和平、造福他人。"丁韪良博士（Dr. W. A. P. Martin）说："这位已经离世的挚爱之人，她那沉静的力量、不倦的奉献和取之不尽的爱心，令我极为敬佩。"她正应验了保罗在腓立比书第二章第三节中的教诲——按原文的意思，保罗劝勉我们"要将自己的生命，如同一方柔软的织锦地毯一般铺展开来，以慈爱的心思、恩慈的同情和乐于助人的服侍，好让他人那疲惫、受伤的双脚，能在路途中寻得一份安舒和慰藉。"唯有神才知道，对许多中国人来说，"人生的道路是何等崎岖坎坷，境遇那尖锐嶙峋的棱角，又是何等狠狠地割伤了他们的双脚。"

Nor did she lose touch with those widely separated from her. In writing to a relative whose marriage had taken her into another denomination, and to a distant home, Mrs. Mateer says: " I would love to see you and your children. It is sad to think of you so far from your friends; the circumstances increase the miles. We do not read the same papers, we do not have the same church acquaintances, we are not interested in the same special meetings. While it is all the Lord's work, our hearts are not so large as His, and able to comprehend so many loves and interests. I have always felt these things to be a bond or a loss as the case may be. Perhaps all this is good for us to draw us nearer our Saviour and away from human helps. I have always been so prone to trust in human arms. Let us do our best in our own little sphere, watching that we do not grow narrow and hard; watching, too, lest we miss some of His leadings to service because it does not come in the line we expected."

她也从未与那些远隔千里之人失去联系。她曾写信给一位因婚姻而改换宗派、又迁居远方的亲戚，说道："我很想见到你和你的孩子们。一想到你远离亲友，我心里就觉得难过；如今的境况，更是让这份距离显得愈发遥远。我们看的不是同样的报纸，认识的教会中人也不一样，感兴趣的特别聚会也各不相同。尽管这一切都是主的工作，但我们的心，终究不及祂那样广大，无法容纳这么多份爱与关切。我一直觉得，这些事，或许会成为一种联结，或许也会成为一种失落，视情况而定。也许，这一切对我们而言反倒是好事，能使我们更加亲近我们的救主，而不再倚靠人的帮助。我一向太容易信赖人的臂膀了。让我们在各自的小天地里，尽力而为吧，留心不要让自己变得狭隘、冷硬；也要留心，不要因为祂的引领并非按我们所预期的方式到来，而错失了祂所要交托给我们的服侍。"

Her judgment was as sound as her sympathies were broad. Her husband felt that her counsel and her praise were always to him beyond price. He consulted her on all occasions, and he was mightily influenced and aided in his work by her quick, unerring judgment and sterling good sense. Indeed, she occupied the place of counsel-general both to the missionaries and to the Chinese. Dr. DuBose says: " In her great work she was all heart, yet keeping a well-balanced head. This is very rare." Another says: " She was blessed with a level head, and characterized by independence and force of character. She had the advantage in her early youth of a thorough grounding in Bible truths, the Shorter Catechism, and the religious works found in the Christian home seventy years ago. She believed with all her heart that ' the Gospel is the power of God unto salvation.' Having made the Bible a lifelong study, she was never troubled with doubts, or with fanciful interpretations. As she believed, so she lived."

她的判断力，正如她的同情心一样宽广而稳健。她的丈夫深深觉得，她的建议和她的赞许，对他而言，向来都是无价之宝。他凡事都要征询她的意见，而她那敏锐、精准的判断力和坚实的常识，也在他的工作中给了他极大的影响和助力。事实上，她实际上扮演着宣教士和中国人共同的总顾问这一角色。杜步西博士（Dr. DuBose）说："在她所成就的伟大事业中，她全然是一颗火热的心，却又始终保持着一个极为平衡的头脑。这实在是十分难得的。"另有一人说："她天生便有一个清明沉稳的头脑，性格独立、意志坚定。她自幼年起，便得益于扎实的圣经真理根基、《西敏小要理问答》，以及七十年前基督教家庭中常见的各类灵修著作的熏陶。她全心全意地相信，'这福音本是神的大能，要救一切相信的'。她把圣经当作一生的功课，因此，她从未被疑惑或那些异想天开的解经所困扰。她信的是什么，活出来的便是什么。"

Indeed, Dr. and Mrs. Mateer together evinced a remarkable and sustained wisdom in their planning and working for the Christian conquest of China, — a wisdom of forty-seven years ago, which the after developments and the present great needs and changes serve only to justify and glorify. They made their aim the building of Christian character, such as would stand by its colors under all circumstances, and constitute the only possible foundation for a renovated and stable empire. In this they chose education, as, for them, the best way to multiply themselves in influence and saving power. With the ability to view a constantly broadening horizon, they gave untiring attention to details and the conquering of small worries. They wrought patiently and quietly for a success that has surely strengthened and justified itself throughout the years.

事实上，狄考文博士夫妇二人，在为基督征服中国而制定的种种规划和所做的工作中，共同展现出一种非凡而持久的智慧——那是四十七年前便已具备的智慧，而此后种种事态的发展、以及当今的种种重大需求与变革，无不进一步证实并彰显了这份智慧的价值。他们所立的目标，是塑造那种无论境遇如何，都能坚守其信仰旗帜的基督徒品格，认为这是重建一个稳定帝国唯一可能的根基。为此，他们选择了教育这条路径，因为在他们看来，这是让自己的影响力和拯救的力量得以成倍增长的最佳途径。他们既有着不断开阔的远见，又肯不厌其烦地关注细节，克服种种琐碎的忧虑。他们以耐心和沉静，默默耕耘，最终成就了一番历经岁月洗礼、愈发彰显其价值的功业。

For both these earnest souls " to live was Christ." The strong and beautiful life sketched in this little volume drew its inspiration from the Cross. From thence came the fragrance of its graces, its abiding cheerfulness in the midst of suffering and exhausting toil, its concentrated and undivided purpose. From thence came also its " burdensome drain of sacrificial desire," and in Christ was its glory and its crown of rejoicing. Could Mrs. Mateer speak to us now, she would say, as her living here always said, " Do all to the glory of God." She would echo the words,

对于这两位赤诚的灵魂而言，"活着就是基督"。本书中所描绘的这段坚强而美好的一生，其灵感的源泉，正是那十字架。正是从那里，涌流出了她一切美德的芬芳、她在痛苦与辛劳交加之中始终不变的欢愉、以及她那专一不移的心志。也正是从那里，涌流出了她那"甘愿背负、乐意献上"的牺牲渴望，而她一切的荣耀与欢欣的冠冕，也全都在基督里面。倘若狄考文夫人此刻能对我们说话，她想必仍会像她在世时那样，说出这句话："凡事都要归荣耀给神。"她也必会应和这样的诗句：

" Yea, through life, death, through sorrow and through sinning
Christ shall suffice thee, for He hath sufficed;
Christ is the end, for Christ was the beginning,
Christ the beginning, for the end is Christ."

"是的，无论生、无论死，无论忧伤，无论罪愆，
基督必将你充足，因祂已然充足；
基督是终点，因基督本是起初，
基督是起初，因终点便是基督。"

原著 1912 年由 Fleming H. Revell Company 出版，已进入公有领域。
